## Comprehensive Save/Load/Persistence System Analysis

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's the complete picture of how the Minecraft-inspired voxel game currently handles persistence:

### 1. **localStorage Keys & Storage Pattern**

**Two independent localStorage keys:**
- **`minecraft_save_v7`** (`SAVE_KEY` in `/lib/game/config.ts:165`) - World saves with all gameplay state
- **`minecraft_audio_v1`** (`AUDIO_SETTINGS_KEY` in `/lib/game/audio/settings.ts:4`) - Audio preferences (separate, not part of world versioning)
- **`minecraft_skin_v1`** (`SKIN_SETTINGS_KEY` in `/lib/game/skinSettings.ts:4`) - Player appearance preference (separate)

The `minecraft_save_v7` suffix indicates this is the 7th iteration of the key, bumped when the **worldgen baseline changes** (not the save schema). Currently at schema version 5.

### 2. **SaveData Schema (Version 5)**

**Location:** `/lib/game/types.ts:115-118`

```
SaveData {
  version: 5                            // Current schema version
  seed: number                          // World seed (deterministic generation)
  changes: Array<[number, number]>      // Block deltas: [voxelIndex, blockId]
  inventorySlots: SavedSlot[]           // 36 slots (9 hotbar + 27 inventory)
  equippedArmor: Partial<EquippedArmor> // Currently equipped pieces
  selectedSlot: number                  // Hotbar selection (0-8)
  player: { x: number; y: number; z: number }  // Player position
  dayClock?: number                     // Time of day (optional)
  hearts?: number                       // Health (1-20, optional)
  hunger?: number                       // Hunger (0-20, optional)
  spawnPoint?: { x, y, z } | null       // Bed respawn point (optional)
  blockEntities?: SavedContainer[]      // Chest contents: { index, slots[] }
  lootedChests?: number[]               // Voxel indices of opened dungeon chests
}
```

### 3. **Block Change System (Differential Storage)**

**Location:** `/lib/game/engine/blockChanges.ts`

The game stores **only block changes** (deltas) from the deterministic worldgen baseline:
- When a block is placed/broken, it's tracked with its voxel index and new BlockId
- If a block is **reverted to its generated state**, it's **pruned from the delta set** (saves space)
- On load, the engine regenerates the world deterministically, then applies saved deltas on top
- The voxel index formula is: `index = x + z*sizeX + y*sizeX*sizeZ` (fixed encoding)

**Critical:** Changing worldgen or the index formula corrupts existing saves → must bump `SAVE_KEY` (already done at v6→v7 for dungeon addition, at v7 for deep-cave lava)

### 4. **Load-on-Startup Flow**

**Location:** `/lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts:125-131`

```
useMinecraftGame() → useCallback(attachMount) → {
  readSave(SAVE_KEY)  // Tries to load at line 130
  → null → new GameEngine({ save: null })  // Fresh world, new random seed
  → SaveData → new GameEngine({ save: readSave(...) })  // Restore from save
}
```

**No "New Game vs Continue" UI yet:**
- Page loads → immediately boots GameEngine
- If `readSave(SAVE_KEY)` returns null, a fresh world is created with `seed = Math.random() * 2^31`
- If a save exists, it's auto-loaded (no menu selection)
- Settings (audio, skin) are read separately on mount (lines 96-106)

**Persistence lifecycle:**
- **On boot:** Calls `readSave(SAVE_KEY)` once (line 130)
- **Every 15 seconds:** `setInterval(autoSave, AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_MS)` (line 188, `AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_MS = 15000` from config.ts:159)
- **On page unload:** `window.addEventListener("beforeunload", autoSave)` (line 189)
- **Manual save:** `saveNow()` hook calls `persistGame()` → `writeSave(SAVE_KEY, api.serialize())` (lines 187, 331)

### 5. **Serialization/Deserialization Code**

**Read path:** `/lib/game/save.ts:75-90`
- `readSave(saveKey, storage)` attempts JSON parse
- Validates: `seed` is finite, `changes` is array
- **Migration chain:** v1 → v2 → v3 → v4 → v5 (lines 81-85)
  - v1→v2: Packs 40 slots into 36, merges stackable items, clamps hotbar
  - v2→v3: Pure bump (time/stats fields added, optional)
  - v3→v4: Pure bump (chest contents added, optional)
  - v4→v5: Pure bump (looted chests tracking added, optional)
- Rejects unknown versions (line 85)

**Write path:** `/lib/game/save.ts:92-94`
- `writeSave(saveKey, data, storage)` → `JSON.stringify(data)`

**Restore helpers:** Lines 96-234
- `restoreInventorySlots()` - Validates items, clamps counts, drops unknown ids, merges legacy `inventoryCounts` format
- `restoreEquippedArmor()` - Validates armor by slot type
- `restoreHearts()`, `restoreHungerLevel()` - Clamps to valid ranges
- `restoreDayClock()` - Validates non-negative finite number
- `restoreSpawnPoint()` - Floors coordinates

**Container (chest) handling:** Lines 122-156
- Only non-empty chests are persisted (`serializeContainers`, line 122)
- Empty chests carry no data (recreated empty on first open)
- On load, entries are validated and matched against actual Chest blocks (GameEngine.ts:164)

**Dungeon looted chests:** Lines 131-140
- `serializeLootedChests()` stores array of voxel indices
- `readLootedChests()` filters non-finite values
- Dungeon chest positions are **re-derived from seed** each load (not persisted) via `collectDungeonSites()` (GameEngine.ts:94)

### 6. **What Is Persisted vs. What Is Transient**

**PERSISTED:**
- World seed
- Block edits (diffs only)
- Player inventory (36 slots with durability)
- Equipped armor
- Selected hotbar slot
- Player position (x, y, z)
- Day clock (time of day)
- Hearts (health)
- Hunger level
- Bed respawn point
- Chest contents (keyed by voxel index)
- Dungeon looted chest indices

**NEVER PERSISTED (transient state):**
- Per-voxel lighting (derived from blocks, recomputed at load via `computeFullLight()`)
- Water/lava exposure timers
- Oxygen/drowning meter
- Mobs and their positions
- In-flight arrows and projectiles
- Thrown spears
- Boss state
- Victory flag
- Weather (cosmetic only, reseed from dayClock on load)
- Crafting/furnace state (transient, not per-block)

### 7. **Settings Storage (Separate from World Saves)**

Audio and appearance are stored in separate keys to avoid versioning the world:
- **`minecraft_audio_v1`:** Master volume, music volume, mute toggle (line 4, settings.ts)
- **`minecraft_skin_v1`:** Selected skin id (line 4, skinSettings.ts)

Both use injectable storage (testable), graceful fallback on parse failure.

### 8. **Versioning & Migration Logic**

**Version history:**
- **v1:** 40 inventory slots, 10-slot hotbar
- **v2:** 36 inventory slots, 9-slot hotbar (legacy migration packs/merges)
- **v3:** Added `dayClock`, `hearts`, `hunger`, `spawnPoint` (optional fields)
- **v4:** Added `blockEntities` for chest contents (optional)
- **v5:** Added `lootedChests` for dungeon loot tracking (optional)

**SAVE_KEY bumps:**
- v6 (dungeon worldgen added) → saves invalidated, old diffs wrong baseline
- v7 (deep-cave lava worldgen added) → saves invalidated again
- Schema stays v5 because lighting is derived + lava is worldgen (not persisted)

Round-trip tests in `/lib/game/save.test.ts` and `/lib/game/engine/GameEngine.test.ts` confirm migrations work.

### 9. **Current Limitations for "Multiple Worlds + Profiles"**

**What would need to change:**

1. **Single active save per key:**
   - Currently: One `minecraft_save_v7` key holds ONE world
   - Needed: Enumeration system (e.g., `minecraft_save_v7_world_1`, `minecraft_save_v7_world_2`)
   - OR: JSON wrapper with metadata array `{ worlds: [{ name, seed, changes, ... }] }`

2. **No UI selection layer:**
   - Currently: Load is automatic or manual-only (pause menu `loadNow()` reloads page)
   - Needed: A "world select" screen at startup (check for multiple saves, list them)
   - OR: Profile/world switcher in pause menu

3. **Player profiles:**
   - Skin is persisted separately (`minecraft_skin_v1`)
   - Inventory/stats are per-world (in the SaveData)
   - Needed: Decouple player name + appearance from world save, store separately

4. **Seed uniqueness:**
   - Currently: Seed is per-save (inside SaveData)
   - Multiple worlds would share app state if they shared a seed (worldgen is deterministic)
   - Each world must have unique seed or unique worldgen baseline

5. **Block diff baseline:**
   - The `changes` array is diffs against **generated terrain**
   - Switching worlds requires switching the block-diff baseline (regenerate)
   - Currently one `blockChangeTracker` per engine, swapping worlds means full engine reload

### 10. **Key File Locations & Line Numbers**

| Purpose | File | Lines |
|---------|------|-------|
| Save key constants | `/lib/game/config.ts` | 165 |
| Audio settings key | `/lib/game/audio/settings.ts` | 4 |
| Skin settings key | `/lib/game/skinSettings.ts` | 4 |
| SaveData types (v1-v5) | `/lib/game/types.ts` | 68-118 |
| Read/write functions | `/lib/game/save.ts` | 75-94 |
| Migration chain | `/lib/game/save.ts` | 16-72 |
| Restore helpers | `/lib/game/save.ts` | 96-234 |
| Block diff tracker | `/lib/game/engine/blockChanges.ts` | 1-54 |
| Load-on-startup flow | `/lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts` | 125-131 |
| Autosave setup | `/lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts` | 187-189 |
| Serialize method | `/lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts` | 387-408 |
| Game engine initialization | `/lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts` | 84-187 |
| Save/load UI (pause menu) | `/components/game/PauseMenu.tsx` | 71-76 |

---

This is a single-world, single-profile system optimized for browser localStorage with full state serialization tied to a deterministic world seed. Implementing multiple worlds + profiles would require adding a world/profile selection layer, enumerating saves by name/id, and potentially refactoring the block-diff baseline system to support world switching without full engine reload.

## Comprehensive Engine / World-State / Game-Lifecycle Analysis

Based on a thorough exploration of your codebase, here's everything you need to understand for the "Multiple Worlds and Multiple Player Profiles" feature:

---

### 1. ENGINE INSTANTIATION & TEARDOWN

**Engine Creation:**
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts` (lines 84–187)
- **Constructor signature:**
  ```typescript
  constructor(options: GameEngineOptions = {}) {
    seed = options.seed ?? Math.random seed (2147483647 range)
    save = options.save ?? null (for restoration)
    rng = options.rng ?? Math.random (injectable randomness)
    worldSize = options.worldSize ?? full size (for tests)
  }
  ```
- **Key initialization steps** (constructor):
  1. Line 88–89: Resolve seed (from save or options or random)
  2. Line 90–91: Create `VoxelWorld` and call `generateWorld(world)` — deterministic from seed
  3. Line 92–94: Call `collectDungeonSites(world)` — re-derives dungeon chest/spawner indices from seed (session-only)
  4. Line 96–97: Create block-change tracker; apply saved block diffs
  5. Line 101: **Bake full lighting** via `computeFullLight(world)` (this is **critical** — lighting is a derived cache, never serialized)
  6. Line 105–152: Initialize player state (position, inventory, health, hunger, oxygen, mobs, timers, etc.)
  7. Line 154–175: If loading a save, restore inventory, armor, position, day clock, loot chest tracking
  8. Line 178–180: Safety check — if player is stuck, force unstuck
  9. Line 182–186: Spawn initial mobs, seed weather, build initial snapshot

**Engine Teardown:**
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts` (lines 258–273)
- **Cleanup on unmount:**
  - Line 262: `minimap?.dispose()` — releases minimap canvas/renders
  - Line 263: `cancelAnimationFrame(animationFrame)` — stop game loop
  - Line 264: `window.clearInterval(autoSaveId)` — stop autosave
  - Line 268: `audioRef.current = null; audio.dispose()` — release WebAudio resources
  - Line 270: `input.dispose()` — remove DOM listeners
  - Line 272: `renderer.dispose()` — **critical**: frees all GPU resources (line 411–429 in GameRenderer.ts)

**Renderer Teardown:**
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/render/GameRenderer.ts` (lines 411–429)
  - Disposes sky, particles, mob/player/projectile visuals
  - Removes meshes from scene, disposes geometries & materials
  - **Critical:** `this.webgl.dispose()` and `mount.removeChild(this.webgl.domElement)` — clears the Three.js context and DOM

**The seam for world switching:** The engine and renderer are independent. You can:
1. Dispose the old renderer (all GPU resources freed)
2. Create a new engine with a different seed/save
3. Create a new renderer pointing to a new DOM mount
4. Hook up the new engine→renderer cycle in the rAF loop

---

### 2. WORLD GENERATION: SEED & DETERMINISM

**Seed Flow:**
- **World creation:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/voxelWorld.ts` (lines 26–33)
  - Constructor takes a `seed` (default 1337)
  - Seed is stored as `this.seed: number` on the world instance
  - **Seed determines everything deterministically** (biomes, terrain height, caves, ores, structures)

**Generation Entry Point:**
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/generation.ts` (lines 67–92)
- **Called once after VoxelWorld creation** (in GameEngine constructor, line 91)
- **Generation order** (critical for determinism):
  1. `generateTerrain(world)` — uses biome map + smooth noise seeded from `world.seed`
  2. `carveCaves(world, rand)` — procedural caves using seeded PRNG
  3. `placeWater(world)` — static sea level + lake logic
  4. `placeBeaches(world)` — sand near water
  5. `placeOres(world, rand)` — ore veins (gates rand on air adjacency, so don't shift RNG)
  6. `placeLava(world)` — deep-cave lava pools (after ores so it doesn't shift their RNG; before dungeons so dungeons overwrite it)
  7. `placeTrees(world, rand)` — tree placement
  8. `placeCacti(world)` — desert cacti
  9. `placeStructures(world, rand)` — villages
  10. `placeDungeons(world)` — underground dungeons with chests & spawners

**Determinism Guarantee:**
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/docs/save-format.md` (lines 2–4, 45–46)
- **Critical invariant:** "Saves store diffs against generated terrain, so changing world generation or the voxel index formula silently corrupts existing saves"
- **Pinned by tests:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/generation.test.ts` — SHA-256 characterization tests verify byte-identical output for known seeds
- **`SAVE_KEY` versioning:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/config.ts` (line 165)
  - Currently `"minecraft_save_v7"`
  - **Bumped when worldgen changes** (e.g., added dungeons v6→v7, added deep-cave lava)
  - Prevents old block-diff saves from being applied to different terrain

**Dungeon Determinism (Key for Profiles):**
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/generation.ts` (lines 487–503)
- **`collectDungeonSites(world)`** — re-derives dungeon chest & spawner voxel indices WITHOUT writing blocks
- **Called every boot** (GameEngine constructor, line 94) to rebuild `dungeonChestIndices` and `dungeonSpawnerIndices` sets
- **Session-only:** These indices are NOT serialized; they're re-derived from seed each load
- **Purpose:** Gates lazy loot fill + spawner activation without storing dungeon metadata

---

### 3. PLAYER STATE MODEL & SEPARABILITY FROM WORLD

**Player State Structure:**
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/state.ts` (lines 16–23, 125–180)
- **PlayerState** (lines 16–23):
  - `position: THREE.Vector3` — XYZ coordinates
  - `velocity: THREE.Vector3`
  - `yaw: number, pitch: number` — look direction (YXZ order)
  - `onGround: boolean`

**Full GameState** (lines 125–180):
  - `player: PlayerState` — above
  - `inventory: InventorySlot[]` — 36 slots (first 9 = hotbar)
  - `equippedArmor: EquippedArmor` — helmet, face_mask, neck, chest, legs, boots
  - `selectedSlot: number` — active hotbar index
  - `hearts: number, hunger: number, oxygen: number` — player stats
  - `isDead: boolean, respawnTimer: number` — death/respawn state
  - `spawnPoint: { x, y, z } | null` — bed respawn point
  - **Container/chest state:**
    - `containers: Map<number, InventorySlot[]>` — chest contents keyed by voxel index
    - `openContainerIndex: number | null` — which chest is open in the inventory
  - **Session-only transient:**
    - `mobs: MobState[], projectiles: ProjectileState[], thrownSpears: ThrownSpearState[]` — never serialized
    - `victory: boolean` — boss defeat flag (session-only)
    - `weather: WeatherState` — cosmetic only, never serialized
    - `sleepTimer: number` — sleeping fade
  - **World reference:**
    - `world: VoxelWorld` — the entire voxel data
    - `blockChanges: BlockChangeTracker` — diff tracker

**Separability: PLAYER IS CLEANLY SEPARABLE**

Player data is **almost entirely independent** of world:
- ✅ Inventory/armor/position/health/hunger — portable across worlds
- ✅ Spawn point (bed respawn) — voxel coordinates, survives world reload
- ❌ **World-coupled data:**
  - `containers` — keyed by voxel index; a different world has different indices
  - `dungeon chest looting state** — indexes into dungeon positions (but see note below)
  - `worldMeshDirty` — renders state (not player state)

**Dungeon Loot Decoupling:**
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/save.ts` (lines 131–140)
- Dungeon chests are filled **lazily** on first access, seeded from `world.seed ^ voxelIndex`
- The persisted `lootedDungeonChests: Set<number>` tracks which indices were looted
- **Separability note:** If player switches to a **different world**, those voxel indices have **no meaning** in the new world (different dungeons exist at different places). Solution: either
  1. Track `(seed, index)` pairs instead of just indices, OR
  2. Reset looted-chest tracking per-world (simpler for "multiple worlds" feature)

- **Critical:** `world.light` is **never serialized**
- **Baked at load:** `computeFullLight(world)` (GameEngine constructor, line 101)
- **Patched on every edit:** `applyEdit(world, world.light, x, y, z)` in blockChanges.set (line 31)
- **Invariant:** If you clone a world, re-bake its lighting; if you swap saves on the same world object, you'll have stale light

**Block-Diff Baseline**
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/blockChanges.ts`
- **Saves store diffs, not absolute blocks**
- **On load:** Regenerate terrain from seed, then apply saved block diffs
- **On edit:** Track baseline vs. current; if reverted to baseline, remove from diff
- **Seam:** If you swap engines mid-session (new seed), the old block-diffs are meaningless in the new world

**World Mesh Rebuild**
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/docs/architecture.md` (lines 19, 72)
- **Triggered when:**
  - Player crosses `RENDER_GRID` boundary
  - `state.worldMeshDirty = true` (block edits, respawn, unstuck)
- **Minimap must sync before renderer** (line 250 in useMinecraftGame.ts) because renderer clears `worldMeshDirty`

**Dungeon Chest Loot (Session-Only Derived)**
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/docs/architecture.md` (lines 63–65)
- **Session state rebuilt each boot:**
  - `dungeonChestIndices` ← `collectDungeonSites(world).chestIndices`
  - `dungeonSpawnerIndices` ← `collectDungeonSites(world).spawnerIndices`
- **Persisted state:**
  - `lootedDungeonChests: Set<number>` — tracks which were already opened
- **Lazy fill:** On first open/break, if index not in looted set, roll loot seeded from `world.seed ^ index`

**Pause Semantics**
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts` (lines 192–195)
- **Full freeze:** Mobs, day clock, mining, stats all stop
- **Snapshot still refreshes** for UI (paused flag, etc.)
- **Seam:** Sleeping is a separate full-freeze (sleepTimer), not the pause flag

---

### 7. SAVE/LOAD MECHANICS FOR PROFILES

**SaveData Structure:**
- **File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/types.ts` (lines 115–118)
- Version 5 contains:
  - `seed: number` — world seed
  - `changes: [voxelIndex, BlockId][]` — block diffs
  - `inventorySlots: SavedSlot[]` — 36 slots
  - `equippedArmor: EquippedArmor` — armor slots
  - `selectedSlot: number`
  - `player: { x, y, z }` — position
  - `dayClock?: number` — time of day (optional, restores with engine default)
  - `hearts?, hunger?` — stats (optional)
  - `spawnPoint?: { x, y, z } | null` — bed respawn (optional)
  - `blockEntities?: SavedContainer[]` — chest contents
  - `lootedChests?: number[]` — dungeon loot tracking

**Save/Load:**
- **Save:** `engine.serialize()` → SaveData → JSON → localStorage[SAVE_KEY]
- **Load:** localStorage[SAVE_KEY] → JSON → `readSave()` → migrate versions → GameEngine constructor
- **Files:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/save.ts`

**Profile Scenario:**
For "multiple worlds + player profiles":
1. **Same world, different profiles:** Save player state separately (inventory/armor/position/stats) with different save keys; world stays the same
2. **Different worlds, same profile:** Save full SaveData (seed + blocks + inventory + position) with different keys; world changes but player state carries over
3. **Different worlds, different profiles:** Save distinct SaveData objects with composite keys (e.g., `${worldId}_${profileId}`)

### CRITICAL INSIGHTS FOR YOUR FEATURE

1. **Engine is completely headless** — can be created/destroyed without React; perfect for swapping worlds
2. **World seed determines everything deterministically** — same seed = identical world every time
3. **Player data is 95% separable** — only dungeon loot tracking (voxel indices) is world-dependent
4. **Block diffs are CRITICAL** — saves store deltas against worldgen, not absolute blocks; you **cannot** apply diffs from world A to world B
5. **Lighting is derived, not persisted** — re-bake after loading any world
6. **Renderer is GPU-resource-heavy** — must be disposed before creating a new one (clears GL context)
7. **SAVE_KEY versioning is essential** — bumped when worldgen changes to prevent corruption
8. **Dungeon indices are session-derived** — `collectDungeonSites()` re-derives them from seed each boot, not persisted in the save
9. **Pause/sleep/death are layer-specific freezes** — each has its own mechanism; respect the step order
10. **Commands are the single mutation surface** — all gameplay intents go through `dispatch()`, making world-switching safe (just drain pending commands before switching)

You have a clean architecture that will support your feature. The main seams to manage are: **engine teardown before switching**, **block-diff validation** (don't mix saves from different seeds), and **dungeon loot tracking** (either reset per world or track (seed, index) pairs).

### 4. GAME START FLOW

**Current flow (the "double-click to start play" commit 85d47eb):**

1. **Initial state:** Engine boots immediately on mount with either:
   - Persisted save from localStorage (via `readSave(SAVE_KEY)`)
   - Or fresh world generation at random spawn point
2. **Click-hint shown:** "Double-click to play" message pulsing (animation: click-hint-pulse, hud.css:261-269)
3. **Double-click triggers:**
   - `onDoubleClick()` in inputController.ts:127-134
   - Calls `canvas.requestPointerLock()`
   - On lock acquisition, `onPointerLockChange` callback (line 143)
   - Sets `locked = true` in React state
4. **After pointer lock:**
   - Click-hint disappears (condition fails: `!locked`)
   - Input is live; mouse look, movement, mining work
   - Simulation advances (paused is false by default)

**Key insight:** There is NO intermediate "New World / Continue / Play" button flow — the engine is running immediately. The double-click is purely a gesture to acquire pointer lock, not a "start" action.

**Current save system (`/lib/game/save.ts`):**

**For multiple worlds, you'd need:**
1. **World index/manifest:**
   ```typescript

     { id: "world-1", name: "Creative", lastPlayed: 1718400000, seed: 12345 },
     { id: "world-2", name: "Survival", lastPlayed: 1718350000, seed: 67890 }
   ])
   ```

2. **World-specific save key:**
   ```typescript
   const getSaveKey = (worldId: string) => `minecraft-save-${worldId}-v6`;
   const save = readSave(getSaveKey(selectedWorldId));
   ```

3. **Profile metadata per world:**
   ```typescript

   SaveData {
     version: 5;
     seed: number;
     changes: [...];

     profile?: {

- **Pre-engine:** `useMinecraftGame` attachment logic (line 125-131)
- **Pause menu:** `PauseMenu.tsx` appearance section (lines 98-114)
- **Save format:** `SaveData` type in `lib/game/types.ts`
- **Input:** `inputController.ts` pause/resume logic (lines 54-58)

---

This architecture is clean: headless engine + React snapshot subscription + single-dispatch command entry point. Adding worlds and profiles requires no refactoring of core systems — just UI layers and localStorage keys.

> AGENT

Great feature to consider — and the good news is your architecture is unusually well-suited to it. Here's my honest take before I ask a couple of decision-critical questions.

## What I think

**Multiple Worlds is a natural fit and low-risk.** Your save layer is already 90% of the way there:
- `readSave(saveKey, …)` / `writeSave(saveKey, …)` in `lib/game/save.ts` *already take a key parameter* — they're just always called with the single `SAVE_KEY` constant (`config.ts:165`). Multi-world is mostly: a world manifest + per-world keys + a selection UI.
- The engine is fully headless and disposable (`GameEngine` + `GameRenderer.dispose()` free everything cleanly), and the mount is a callback-ref. So switching worlds can be done by unmounting/remounting the game subtree with a React `key={worldId}` — **no page reload, no engine refactor.** The existing teardown path already handles it.
- A world is fully defined by its `seed` + block diffs, so worlds are completely independent. Just don't mix diffs across seeds (the save layer already guards seed validity).

**Multiple Player Profiles is where the real design decision lives** — and it's a *product* decision, not a technical one, because it changes the data model significantly. The key question is the relationship between profiles and worlds:

- If **a profile owns its worlds** (like user accounts on a shared console), it's clean and cheap: `SaveData` stays one bundled blob per world, appearance/skin moves onto the profile, and selecting a profile just filters the world list. This matches your headless "one engine = one world" model perfectly.
- If **profiles are reusable characters you carry into any shared world**, it's a *much* bigger refactor: today `SaveData` bundles player state (inventory, position, stats, armor) *and* world state (block diffs, containers, dungeon loot) into one blob. You'd have to split those and key player-state by `(world, profile)` — plus answer awkward questions like "do two characters in the same world share block edits?"

I'd strongly recommend the first model. But this is your call, so let me confirm the direction before I write the plan.

199	    // time; the cap bounds work per frame and quietly drops time beyond it

5	/**
6	 * Migrates a v1 save (40 slots, 10-slot hotbar) to v2 (36 slots, 9-slot
7	 * hotbar): non-empty slots are packed in order, stackable items merge into
8	 * earlier stacks, and anything that still overflows 36 slots is dropped.
9	 */
10	/** Coerces a persisted hotbar index to a finite integer within 0..HOTBAR_SLOTS-1. */
11	function normalizeSelectedSlot(value: number): number {
12	  const index = Number.isFinite(value) ? Math.floor(value) : 0;
13	  return Math.max(0, Math.min(HOTBAR_SLOTS - 1, index));
14	}
15	
16	export function migrateSaveV1toV2(save: SaveDataV1): SaveDataV2 {
17	  const migrated: SaveDataV2 = { ...save, version: 2, selectedSlot: normalizeSelectedSlot(save.selectedSlot) };
18	
19	  if (Array.isArray(save.inventorySlots)) {
20	    const packed: Array<{ id: string | null; count: number; durability?: number }> = [];
21	    for (const saved of save.inventorySlots) {
22	      if (!saved?.id || saved.count <= 0) continue;
23	      // Items without durability stack; merge them into an earlier stack first.
24	      const stackable = ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[saved.id] ? !ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[saved.id].maxDurability : false;
25	      let remaining = saved.count;
26	      if (stackable) {
27	        for (const slot of packed) {
28	          if (slot.id !== saved.id || slot.count >= MAX_STACK_SIZE) continue;
29	          const moved = Math.min(MAX_STACK_SIZE - slot.count, remaining);
30	          slot.count += moved;
31	          remaining -= moved;
32	          if (remaining === 0) break;
33	        }
34	      }
35	      while (remaining > 0 && packed.length < INVENTORY_SLOTS) {
36	        const moved = Math.min(maxStackSizeForItem(saved.id), remaining);
37	        packed.push({ id: saved.id, count: moved, durability: saved.durability });
38	        remaining -= moved;
39	      }
40	    }
41	    migrated.inventorySlots = packed;
42	  }
43	
44	  return migrated;
45	}
46	
47	/**
48	 * Migrates a v2 save to v3 — a pure version bump. The new persisted fields
49	 * (dayClock, hearts, hunger, spawnPoint) are optional, so an older save simply
50	 * loads with the engine's defaults for them.
51	 */
52	export function migrateSaveV2toV3(save: SaveDataV2): SaveDataV3 {
53	  return { ...save, version: 3 };
54	}
55	
56	/**
57	 * Migrates a v3 save to v4 — a pure version bump. `blockEntities` (chest
58	 * contents) is optional, so a pre-chest save simply loads with no containers.
59	 */

61	  return { ...save, version: 4 };
62	}
63	
64	/**

66	 * so a pre-dungeon save simply loads with no dungeon chests yet looted. (In
67	 * practice the SAVE_KEY bump to v6 discards pre-dungeon saves, but the
68	 * migration keeps the version chain complete and the readers total.)
69	 */
70	export function migrateSaveV4toV5(save: SaveDataV4): SaveData {
71	  return { ...save, version: 5 };
72	}

74	// Storage is injectable so save logic can be tested without a browser.
75	export function readSave(saveKey: string, storage: Storage = localStorage): SaveData | null {
76	  try {
77	    const raw = storage.getItem(saveKey);
78	    if (!raw) return null;
79	    const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as SaveData | SaveDataV4 | SaveDataV3 | SaveDataV2 | SaveDataV1;
80	    if (!parsed || !Number.isFinite(parsed.seed) || !Array.isArray(parsed.changes)) return null;
81	    let migrated: SaveDataV2 | SaveDataV3 | SaveDataV4 | SaveData = parsed.version === 1 ? migrateSaveV1toV2(parsed) : parsed;
82	    if (migrated.version === 2) migrated = migrateSaveV2toV3(migrated);
83	    if (migrated.version === 3) migrated = migrateSaveV3toV4(migrated);
84	    if (migrated.version === 4) migrated = migrateSaveV4toV5(migrated);
85	    if (migrated.version !== 5) return null;
86	    return migrated;
87	  } catch {
88	    return null;
89	  }
90	}

96	export function inventorySlotsSnapshot(inventory: InventorySlot[]): SavedSlot[] {
97	  return inventory.map((slot) => ({ id: slot.id, count: slot.count, durability: slot.durability }));
98	}
99	
100	/**
101	 * Rebuilds one slot from its saved snapshot: drops unknown ids, clamps the
102	 * count, and clamps/defaults durability for gear (a non-positive saved
103	 * durability means the piece broke and is dropped). Returns an empty slot for
104	 * anything invalid. Shared by inventory and container restore.
105	 */
106	function restoreSlot(saved: SavedSlot | undefined): InventorySlot {
107	  if (!saved?.id || saved.count <= 0 || !ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[saved.id]) return createEmptySlot();
108	  const slot = createSlot(saved.id, Math.min(maxStackSizeForItem(saved.id), Math.max(0, Math.floor(saved.count))));
109	  if ((slot.kind === "tool" || slot.kind === "weapon" || slot.kind === "armor") && slot.maxDurability) {
110	    if (typeof saved.durability === "number") {
111	      const loaded = Math.floor(saved.durability);
112	      if (loaded <= 0) return createEmptySlot();
113	      slot.durability = Math.max(1, Math.min(slot.maxDurability, loaded));
114	    } else {
115	      slot.durability = slot.maxDurability;
116	    }
117	  }
118	  return slot;
119	}
120	
121	/** Snapshots non-empty chest containers for persistence (empty chests carry no data). */
122	export function serializeContainers(containers: Map<number, InventorySlot[]>): SavedContainer[] {
123	  const out: SavedContainer[] = [];
124	  for (const [index, slots] of containers) {
125	    if (!slots.some((slot) => slot.id && slot.count > 0)) continue;
126	    out.push({ index, slots: inventorySlotsSnapshot(slots) });
127	  }
128	  return out;
129	}
130	
131	/** Snapshots the set of opened/broken dungeon chest voxel indices for persistence. */
132	export function serializeLootedChests(looted: Set<number>): number[] {
133	  return [...looted];
134	}
135	
136	/** Reads the opened/broken dungeon chest indices from a save (finite numbers only). */
137	export function readLootedChests(save: SaveData): number[] {
138	  if (!Array.isArray(save.lootedChests)) return [];
139	  return save.lootedChests.filter((value) => Number.isFinite(value));
140	}
141	
142	/**
143	 * Reads chest containers from a save into validated, CHEST_SLOTS-length slot
144	 * arrays keyed by voxel index. Per-slot validation only — the engine still
145	 * confirms each index actually holds a Chest block before using it.
146	 */
147	export function readContainers(save: SaveData): Array<{ index: number; slots: InventorySlot[] }> {
148	  if (!Array.isArray(save.blockEntities)) return [];
149	  const out: Array<{ index: number; slots: InventorySlot[] }> = [];
150	  for (const entry of save.blockEntities) {
151	    if (!entry || !Number.isFinite(entry.index) || !Array.isArray(entry.slots)) continue;
152	    const slots = Array.from({ length: CHEST_SLOTS }, (_, i) => restoreSlot(entry.slots[i]));
153	    out.push({ index: entry.index, slots });
154	  }
155	  return out;
156	}
157	
158	/**
159	 * Rebuilds inventory slots from a save, dropping unknown items, clamping
160	 * counts and durability, and skipping broken gear. Supports both the current
161	 * inventorySlots shape and the legacy inventoryCounts shape. Returns null when
162	 * the save carries no inventory.
163	 */
164	export function restoreInventorySlots(save: SaveData): InventorySlot[] | null {
165	  if (Array.isArray(save.inventorySlots)) {
166	    const slots = Array.from({ length: INVENTORY_SLOTS }, () => createEmptySlot());
167	    for (let i = 0; i < Math.min(INVENTORY_SLOTS, save.inventorySlots.length); i += 1) {
168	      slots[i] = restoreSlot(save.inventorySlots[i]);
169	    }
170	    return slots;
171	  }
172	
173	  if (save.inventoryCounts) {
174	    const slots = Array.from({ length: INVENTORY_SLOTS }, () => createEmptySlot());
175	    let cursor = 0;
176	    for (const [id, raw] of Object.entries(save.inventoryCounts)) {
177	      if (!ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[id]) continue;
178	      let remaining = Math.max(0, Math.floor(raw));
179	      while (remaining > 0 && cursor < slots.length) {
180	        const add = Math.min(maxStackSizeForItem(id), remaining);
181	        slots[cursor] = createSlot(id, add);
182	        cursor += 1;
183	        remaining -= add;
184	      }
185	    }
186	    return slots;
187	  }
188	
189	  return null;
190	}
191	
192	/** Restores equipped armor from a save, ignoring ids that are not valid armor for the slot. */
193	export function restoreEquippedArmor(save: SaveData): EquippedArmor | null {
194	  if (!save.equippedArmor) return null;
195	  const next = createEmptyArmorEquipment();
196	  for (const armorSlot of ARMOR_SLOTS) {
197	    const equippedId = save.equippedArmor[armorSlot];
198	    if (!equippedId) continue;
199	    const def = ITEM_DEF_BY_ID[equippedId];
200	    if (def?.kind !== "armor" || def.armorSlot !== armorSlot) continue;
201	    next[armorSlot] = equippedId;
202	  }
203	  return next;
204	}
205	
206	export function restoreSelectedSlot(save: SaveData): number | null {
207	  if (typeof save.selectedSlot !== "number") return null;
208	  return normalizeSelectedSlot(save.selectedSlot);
209	}
210	
211	/** Restores the day clock from a save (finite, non-negative); null if absent/invalid. */
212	export function restoreDayClock(save: SaveData): number | null {
213	  if (typeof save.dayClock !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(save.dayClock) || save.dayClock < 0) return null;
214	  return save.dayClock;
215	}
216	
217	/** Restores hearts clamped to 1..MAX_HEARTS; null if absent/invalid. */
218	export function restoreHearts(save: SaveData): number | null {
219	  if (typeof save.hearts !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(save.hearts)) return null;
220	  return Math.max(1, Math.min(MAX_HEARTS, Math.floor(save.hearts)));
221	}
222	
223	/** Restores hunger clamped to 0..MAX_HUNGER; null if absent/invalid. */
224	export function restoreHungerLevel(save: SaveData): number | null {
225	  if (typeof save.hunger !== "number" || !Number.isFinite(save.hunger)) return null;
226	  return Math.max(0, Math.min(MAX_HUNGER, Math.floor(save.hunger)));
227	}
228	
229	/** Restores the bed respawn point; null if absent or explicitly cleared. */
230	export function restoreSpawnPoint(save: SaveData): { x: number; y: number; z: number } | null {
231	  const sp = save.spawnPoint;
232	  if (!sp || !Number.isFinite(sp.x) || !Number.isFinite(sp.y) || !Number.isFinite(sp.z)) return null;
233	  return { x: Math.floor(sp.x), y: Math.floor(sp.y), z: Math.floor(sp.z) };
234	}

---

# Implementation Plan: Multiple Worlds and Multiple Player Profiles

## 0. Confirmation of your assumptions (all verified against the code)

Everything you stated checks out. Specifics I confirmed:

- `readSave(saveKey, storage=localStorage)` / `writeSave(saveKey, data, storage=localStorage)` already take a key param (`lib/game/save.ts:75,92`). Both validate the parsed blob and run the v1→v5 migration chain. Reusing them per-world is trivial.
- `SAVE_KEY = "minecraft_save_v7"` at `lib/game/config.ts:165`. `serialize()` writes `version: 5` and includes `seed` (`GameEngine.ts:387-408`).
- `GameEngine` constructor (`GameEngine.ts:84-90`): `seed = save?.seed ?? options.seed ?? Math.floor(Math.random() * 2147483647)`. So passing `{ save }` with a seed restores it; a fresh world with an explicit seed needs `{ seed }`; omitting both is random. This is the entropy expression to reuse.
- `attachMount` (`useMinecraftGame.ts:125-131`) is a callback ref that calls `new GameEngine({ save: readSave(SAVE_KEY) })` and `setCtx`. The big effect keyed on `[ctx, flashMessage]` (lines 150-274) owns renderer/input/audio/rAF/autosave and disposes everything in cleanup. This is exactly what makes a React `key` remount work.
- Skin read at line 100-104 via `readSkinSettings()`; `updateSkin` (116-121) writes `minecraft_skin_v1` and calls `rendererRef.current?.setPlayerSkin(...)`.
- `loadNow`/`resetNow` (334-350) do `window.location.reload()` / `localStorage.removeItem(SAVE_KEY)` + reload.
- No title screen: `app/page.tsx` → `MinecraftGame`. Pause menu has Back/Save/Load/Reset/Sound/Skins/Controls.
- Audio settings: `minecraft_audio_v1`, injectable storage, default-on-corrupt pattern (`audio/settings.ts`). This stays global, untouched.
- Tests: bun picks up co-located `*.test.ts(x)` (preload `tests/setup.ts`, happy-dom); Playwright is `e2e/**/*.e2e.ts` only. `fakeStorage()` helper pattern is in `skinSettings.test.ts`. The smoke e2e hardcodes `"minecraft_save_v7"` and `"minecraft_skin_v1"` in `localStorage.setItem/getItem` calls — those will need updating (flagged in §7/§9).

**One contradiction to flag** (minor): you suggested `minecraft_save_v7_<worldId>` as the per-world key. The `_v7` worldgen suffix is awkward to embed mid-key because the suffix is a *worldgen baseline* version that gets bumped independently of the schema. I recommend embedding the version differently — see §1 — so a future worldgen bump doesn't force you to migrate every world's manifest entry. Net: keep your scheme but make the worldgen version a *field on the world record*, not part of the key prefix. I'll lay out both and recommend one.

---

## 1. localStorage data model

### Keys overview

| Key | Owner | Shape | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `minecraft_profiles_v1` | `lib/game/profiles.ts` | `ProfilesManifest` | profile list + activeProfileId |
| `minecraft_worlds_v1` | `lib/game/worlds.ts` | `WorldsManifest` | world index across all profiles |
| `minecraft_world_save_<worldId>` | per-world (via existing `readSave`/`writeSave`) | `SaveData` (v5) | one bundled blob per world |
| `minecraft_audio_v1` | unchanged | `AudioSettings` | GLOBAL, shared |
| `minecraft_skin_v1` | legacy only | `SkinSettings` | consumed once by migration, then orphaned (see §2) |
| `minecraft_save_v7` | legacy only | `SaveData` | consumed once by migration |

### Versioned manifest wrapper

Both manifests share a tiny envelope so they can migrate independently of their payload, mirroring the `SaveData.version` discipline:

```ts
// lib/game/manifest.ts  (shared helper, NEW)
export type Manifest<TVersion extends number, TData> = { version: TVersion } & TData;

// Generic guarded reader/writer factory so profiles.ts and worlds.ts don't
// each re-implement try/catch + version gating. Storage injectable for tests.
export function readManifest<T>(key: string, parse: (raw: unknown) => T | null,
  fallback: () => T, storage?: Storage): T;
export function writeManifest(key: string, value: unknown, storage?: Storage): void; // swallows quota
```

(Optional — if you'd rather not add a 4th module, inline the try/catch in each. I'd add `manifest.ts`; it keeps `profiles.ts`/`worlds.ts` lean and is the natural home for the future migration switch.)

### Profile manifest — `minecraft_profiles_v1`

```ts
// lib/game/profiles.ts
export type Profile = {
  id: string;            // crypto.randomUUID()
  name: string;          // display name, 1..24 chars after trim
  skinId: SkinId;        // validated via isSkinId; replaces the global skin read
  createdAt: number;     // Date.now()
};

export type ProfilesManifest = {
  version: 1;
  profiles: Profile[];
  activeProfileId: string | null;  // null => show profile-select on boot
};

export const PROFILES_KEY = "minecraft_profiles_v1";
export const DEFAULT_PROFILES_MANIFEST: ProfilesManifest =
  { version: 1, profiles: [], activeProfileId: null };
```

CRUD surface (pure, storage-injectable, each returns the new manifest and persists):

```ts
readProfiles(storage?): ProfilesManifest
createProfile(name: string, skinId: SkinId, storage?): Profile          // appends, returns the new profile
renameProfile(id, name, storage?): ProfilesManifest
setProfileSkin(id, skinId, storage?): ProfilesManifest                  // used by pause-menu skin swatches
deleteProfile(id, storage?): ProfilesManifest                          // also deletes its worlds + save blobs (see §7)
setActiveProfile(id: string | null, storage?): ProfilesManifest
getActiveProfile(storage?): Profile | null
```

Validation on read (total reader, never throws): drop entries without a string `id`/`name`; coerce `skinId` via `isSkinId` → fallback `DEFAULT_SKIN_ID`; if `activeProfileId` doesn't match any surviving profile, set it to the first profile's id (or `null` if none).

### World manifest — `minecraft_worlds_v1`

```ts
// lib/game/worlds.ts
export type WorldMeta = {
  id: string;            // crypto.randomUUID(); the per-world save key is derived from this
  profileId: string;     // owning profile (the 1:N link)
  name: string;          // "My World", 1..32 chars after trim
  seed: number;          // resolved integer seed (see seed rules below)
  worldgenVersion: number; // 7 today (mirrors the numeric part of SAVE_KEY); see seed/worldgen note
  createdAt: number;
  lastPlayedAt: number;  // bumped on entering play; drives default ordering
};

export type WorldsManifest = {
  version: 1;
  worlds: WorldMeta[];
};

export const WORLDS_KEY = "minecraft_worlds_v1";
export const WORLD_SAVE_PREFIX = "minecraft_world_save_";

/** The per-world SaveData key handed to readSave/writeSave. */
export function worldSaveKey(worldId: string): string {
  return WORLD_SAVE_PREFIX + worldId;   // e.g. minecraft_world_save_3f2a...
}
```

CRUD surface:

```ts
readWorlds(storage?): WorldsManifest
worldsForProfile(profileId, storage?): WorldMeta[]                 // filtered + sorted by lastPlayedAt desc
createWorld(profileId, name, seedInput: string | null, storage?): WorldMeta
renameWorld(id, name, storage?): WorldsManifest
touchWorld(id, storage?): WorldsManifest                           // sets lastPlayedAt = Date.now()
deleteWorld(id, storage?): WorldsManifest                          // caller also removes worldSaveKey blob
getWorld(id, storage?): WorldMeta | null
```

### Seed selection & storage

`createWorld(profileId, name, seedInput)` resolves the seed once, at creation, and stores the resolved integer in `WorldMeta.seed` (so the world is reproducible and the manifest is the single source of truth — the engine no longer rolls a random seed for a fresh world):

```ts
function resolveSeed(seedInput: string | null): number {
  const trimmed = (seedInput ?? "").trim();
  if (trimmed === "") return Math.floor(Math.random() * 2147483647);   // same entropy as GameEngine today
  const asNumber = Number(trimmed);
  if (Number.isFinite(asNumber)) return Math.floor(Math.abs(asNumber)) % 2147483647;
  // Non-numeric text seed (Minecraft-style): hash to an int (stable djb2/FNV).
  return hashStringToSeed(trimmed);   // small pure helper in worlds.ts
}
```

Then the engine boots a fresh world with `new GameEngine({ seed: world.seed })` (no save yet), and on subsequent visits with `new GameEngine({ save: readSave(worldSaveKey(world.id)) })`. Because the very first autosave/serialize writes `seed` into the blob, and `WorldMeta.seed` already holds the same value, the two never diverge.

**`worldgenVersion` rationale (the contradiction fix):** Store today's `7` on each `WorldMeta`. The per-world *save key* stays version-free (`minecraft_world_save_<id>`). When worldgen changes (the thing that historically bumped `SAVE_KEY`), you compare `world.worldgenVersion` to the current `WORLDGEN_VERSION` constant at load time: if they differ, that world's block-diffs are stale, so you discard the save blob (boot fresh from the stored seed) and update the record — exactly the semantics the `_v7` bump gives today, but per-world and without a key migration. This is strictly better than baking `_v7` into the key. If you prefer to keep your literal `minecraft_save_v7_<worldId>` scheme instead, it also works; you just lose per-world granularity and must rewrite every key on a future bump. **Recommendation: version-as-field.**

---

## 2. Migration of the legacy single save

Implement `migrateLegacySave(storage = localStorage): void` in a new `lib/game/legacyMigration.ts`, called **once** from the menu shell on first mount (before reading manifests for display).

Idempotency gate: run only when `minecraft_profiles_v1` is **absent**. Presence of the profiles manifest is the "migration already done" flag — never key off the legacy save's presence, because a user could legitimately have both during a rollback.

```ts
export function migrateLegacySave(storage: Storage = localStorage): void {
  if (storage.getItem(PROFILES_KEY)) return;          // already migrated / fresh post-feature install

  const legacy = readSave(SAVE_KEY_LEGACY, storage);  // "minecraft_save_v7", runs v1->v5 chain
  const legacySkin = readSkinSettings(storage).skinId; // global skin becomes the default profile's skin

  const profile = createProfile("Player", legacySkin, storage); // writes minecraft_profiles_v1 + sets active

  if (legacy) {
    const world = createWorld(profile.id, "My World", String(legacy.seed), storage); // worldgenVersion: 7
    writeSave(worldSaveKey(world.id), legacy, storage); // COPY the blob to the per-world key
  }

  setActiveProfile(profile.id, storage);              // so boot lands on world-select for that profile
}
```

Decisions:
- **Copy, don't move-by-reference.** `writeSave` the legacy blob to the new key. Keep it a copy so a half-migration (quota failure mid-way) can't lose data.
- **Delete the legacy `minecraft_save_v7` key?** Yes, but only after the per-world write succeeds — `storage.removeItem(SAVE_KEY_LEGACY)` at the end of the `if (legacy)` block, wrapped so a removal failure is non-fatal. This prevents the e2e/older code paths from re-reading a stale single save and avoids quota pressure. *(If you want to be conservative, leave it for one release and delete in a follow-up — but deleting now is cleaner and the data is safely copied.)*
- **Leave `minecraft_skin_v1` in place.** It's only *read* during migration to seed the default profile's skin; it's harmless to leave. Per-profile skin now lives in the profile record. The pause-menu skin swatches write to the active profile, not to `minecraft_skin_v1`, so the legacy key simply goes stale. (Deleting it is optional and low-value; I'd leave it to keep migration minimal.)
- **No legacy save but profiles absent** (brand-new player): `createProfile("Player", default skin)` only; zero worlds; boot lands on that profile's empty world-select with a "Create World" prompt. Equally you can land on profile-select — see §3 default-routing.

A standalone `lib/game/legacyMigration.test.ts` covers all four branches (legacy+skin, legacy no custom skin, no legacy, already-migrated no-op).

---

## 3. React shell / menu state machine

### Where the state lives — split `app/page.tsx`, not `MinecraftGame`

Introduce a **menu shell** that sits above `MinecraftGame`. `MinecraftGame` stays almost exactly as-is (it becomes "the running game for one world"), which keeps the giant `useEffect` lifecycle untouched and lets the React `key` do the world-switch.

New top-level client component: `components/GameShell.tsx` (rendered by `app/page.tsx` instead of `MinecraftGame`). It owns the screen state machine and the active world/profile.

```ts
type Screen =
  | { name: "profile-select" }
  | { name: "world-select"; profileId: string }
  | { name: "play"; profileId: string; worldId: string };
```

Transitions:
- boot → run `migrateLegacySave()` once (effect), then route: if `activeProfileId` set and that profile has ≥1 world → could go straight to `world-select` for it; default landing is `profile-select` if >1 profile, else `world-select` for the lone/active profile. Simplest deterministic rule: **always land on `profile-select`** (Minecraft-console-like). Pick one; I recommend `profile-select` for clarity, with the active profile preselected/highlighted.
- `profile-select`: pick a profile → `world-select`; create/rename/delete profile flows stay on this screen; "Play" on a profile sets it active.
- `world-select`: pick a world → `touchWorld(id)` then `play`; create world (name + optional seed) → append → optionally auto-enter; rename/delete world stay on this screen; "Back" → `profile-select`.
- `play`: renders `<MinecraftGame key={worldId} worldId={worldId} profile={profile} onQuitToWorlds={...} onQuitToTitle={...} />`.

### Runtime world switch WITHOUT reload — the `key` trick

Render the play screen as:

```tsx
{screen.name === "play" && (
  <MinecraftGame
    key={screen.worldId}            // <-- the whole engine/renderer subtree remounts on world change
    worldId={screen.worldId}
    profile={activeProfile}
    onQuitToWorlds={() => setScreen({ name: "world-select", profileId: screen.profileId })}
  />
)}
```

When the user picks a different world, `screen.worldId` changes, React unmounts the old `MinecraftGame` subtree. That unmount runs the cleanup in the `[ctx, flashMessage]` effect (`useMinecraftGame.ts:258-273`) which `dispose()`s renderer/audio/input, clears the rAF + autosave interval, and removes the `beforeunload` listener. A fresh mount then boots the new world. **No `window.location.reload` anywhere** — `loadNow`/`resetNow` get rewritten to drive this same remount (see §4). This is the central architectural move and it requires zero changes to the engine or the big effect.

"Quit to worlds" from the pause menu calls `onQuitToWorlds()` (a new prop). Before leaving, the game must persist (autosave fires on unmount? No — `beforeunload` only fires on tab close, and the interval is cleared in cleanup). So **the pause-menu "Quit" handler must call `saveNow()` synchronously before invoking `onQuitToWorlds`** (see §4/§7 — autosave correctness).

### Components to add (see §5 for full list)
`GameShell.tsx` (state machine) plus `components/menu/ProfileSelect.tsx`, `WorldSelect.tsx`, `CreateProfileForm.tsx`, `CreateWorldForm.tsx`, `MenuButton`/list primitives as needed.

---

## 4. Wiring `useMinecraftGame` to the selected world + profile

`useMinecraftGame` gains parameters; `MinecraftGame` passes them through.

```ts
export function useMinecraftGame(opts: {
  worldId: string;
  profile: Profile;                 // name + skinId
  onQuitToWorlds: () => void;
}) { ... }
```

Concrete changes inside the hook:

1. **Save key.** Replace the module-level `SAVE_KEY` usages with a per-world key. Compute once: `const saveKey = worldSaveKey(opts.worldId);` Since the hook instance is tied to a `key={worldId}` mount, `saveKey` is stable for the component's life — capture it in a ref (`saveKeyRef`) so the long-lived effect/closures read the right key even though they were created once.
   - `attachMount` (125-131): `new GameEngine({ save: readSave(saveKeyRef.current) })`. If `readSave` returns `null` (fresh world), pass `{ seed: world.seed }` instead — so `attachMount` needs the seed too. Easiest: pass `{ save: readSave(key) ?? undefined, seed: world.seed }`; the engine prefers `save?.seed` when a save exists and falls back to `seed` for a fresh world (`GameEngine.ts:88`). So **always pass both** `save` and `seed` and let the engine's precedence do the right thing. Thread `world.seed` in as a prop too (or pass the whole `WorldMeta`).

2. **`persistGame` / autosave / `saveNow`.** `persistGame(api, key, onMessage)` gains the key param; the module-level helper currently hardcodes `SAVE_KEY` (line 71). Autosave at 187-189 calls `persistGame(gameEngine, saveKeyRef.current, flashMessage)`. `saveNow` (331-333) likewise. **Critical:** the autosave closure is created inside the effect that runs once per mount; because the key is per-mount-stable, reading `saveKeyRef.current` guarantees autosave writes the right world even right after a switch (the old mount's interval is already cleared before the new one starts).

3. **`loadNow` (334-341):** no more reload. "Load" = discard the current in-memory session and re-read the on-disk save for *this* world. Cleanest implementation that reuses the `key` machinery: lift a "remount nonce" into `GameShell` is overkill; instead **route Load through `onQuitToWorlds` is wrong** (that leaves the world). Better: have `GameShell` own a `playNonce` and pass `key={`${worldId}:${playNonce}`}`; `loadNow` calls a new prop `onReloadWorld()` that bumps the nonce → remount → `attachMount` re-reads the saved blob. If no save exists for the key, flash "No save found" (same guard, using `readSave(saveKeyRef.current)`).

4. **`resetNow` (342-350):** "Reset World" = wipe this world's blob and reboot from its stored seed. `localStorage.removeItem(saveKeyRef.current)` then `onReloadWorld()` (nonce bump) — the fresh mount finds no save and boots from `world.seed`. The seed stays in the manifest, so a reset world keeps its identity/name/seed (matches "Reset World", not "delete"). Flash "Resetting…".

5. **Skin source.** Replace `readSkinSettings()` (line 100) with `opts.profile.skinId`. Seed `skinIdRef.current` / `setSkinId` from the profile. `updateSkin` (116-121) must now: call `setProfileSkin(opts.profile.id, id)` (persist to the profile manifest) **instead of** `writeSkinSettings`, and still `rendererRef.current?.setPlayerSkin(...)`. The pause-menu swatch grid keeps working; it now edits the active profile. (Optional nicety: also bubble the change up so `GameShell`'s in-memory profile copy updates, but since the player model updates live via `setPlayerSkin`, a refresh-on-next-menu-visit is fine.)

6. **Audio stays global.** No change to `readAudioSettings`/`writeAudioSettings`/`updateAudioSettings` (lines 97-99, 108-114). They remain on `minecraft_audio_v1`.

7. **`beforeunload` autosave (189):** unchanged in mechanism, but now writes to the per-world key via the same `persistGame(..., saveKeyRef.current, ...)`. Correct because each mount registers/cleans up its own listener.

8. **Quit-to-worlds save.** Add a `quitToWorlds()` returned by the hook that does `if (engine) persistGame(engine, saveKeyRef.current, flashMessage); opts.onQuitToWorlds();`. The pause menu's new "Quit to Worlds" button calls it. This guarantees a save before the world-switch remount tears the engine down (the cleared interval won't fire, and `beforeunload` won't fire on an in-app navigation).

## 7. Edge cases & risks

- **Delete the *active* world while playing:** Not directly reachable — you can only delete from `world-select`, which means you're not in `play`. But "Quit to Worlds" then delete is fine. When deleting any world, also `removeItem(worldSaveKey(id))` to reclaim quota.
- **Last world of a profile deleted:** `world-select` shows the empty state ("Create your first world"). Active profile stays valid.
- **Last profile deleted:** After delete, if zero profiles remain, route to a "create first profile" screen (don't allow a profile-less state to play). `setActiveProfile(null)`.
- **Delete a profile owns N worlds:** `deleteProfile` must cascade — enumerate `worldsForProfile(id)`, `removeItem` each save blob, remove them from the worlds manifest, then remove the profile. Do the cascade *before* removing the profile so a mid-way failure leaves a recoverable state.
- **localStorage quota:** A world blob can be large (block-diffs). All manifest writers swallow quota errors (the existing skin/audio pattern). For per-world `writeSave` a quota failure currently throws inside `persistGame`'s try (line 69-74) → flashes "Save failed" — keep that. Deleting worlds/profiles frees space. Document the discard-on-`worldgenVersion`-mismatch as also reclaiming space.
- **`lastPlayedAt` ordering:** set on entering `play` (`touchWorld`), so the most recently played world floats to the top of `world-select`. New worlds get `lastPlayedAt = createdAt` so they appear at top until played. Ties broken by `createdAt` desc.
- **Autosave writing to the right key after a switch:** Guaranteed by `key={worldId}` remount — the old interval is `clearInterval`'d in cleanup (line 264) *before* the new mount's effect installs a fresh interval reading the new `saveKeyRef`. No shared mutable interval across worlds. The "Quit to Worlds" path additionally forces a synchronous save (§4.8) because the interval won't fire on an in-app switch.
- **`beforeunload` save:** still works per-mount; writes the active world's key. On a world *switch* (not tab close) `beforeunload` doesn't fire, hence the explicit quit-save.
- **Pointer-lock "Double-click to play" vs menus:** The menus are plain DOM over no canvas (the canvas only exists in `play`). `showClickHint` (`MinecraftGame.tsx:83`) only renders inside `play`, so there's no conflict. When entering a world, the player lands unlocked with the existing "Double-click to play" hint — unchanged UX. When returning from pause "Quit to Worlds", `document.exitPointerLock()` already runs in effect cleanup (line 271), so the cursor is free for the menu. Good.
- **Skin divergence:** `updateSkin` now writes the profile, not `minecraft_skin_v1`. Ensure `CreateProfileForm` and `PauseMenu` both go through the same `setProfileSkin`/profile state so a skin chosen at creation and one chosen mid-game agree. The in-memory `profile` prop passed to a *currently mounted* game won't auto-update from a manifest write — but the renderer is updated imperatively via `setPlayerSkin`, so it's visually correct; the menu re-reads the manifest on next visit.
- **`activeProfileId` pointing at a deleted profile** (e.g. manual localStorage edit): `readProfiles` repairs it to the first profile or `null`.
- **Migration race / double-mount (React 18/19 StrictMode dev):** `migrateLegacySave` is idempotent (gated on profiles-manifest presence), so a double-invoke in dev StrictMode is safe.

---

## 8. Commit slicing (Conventional Commits, lint/build green at each step)

1. **`feat(save): add versioned profile + world manifests`** — `lib/game/manifest.ts`, `lib/game/profiles.ts`, `lib/game/worlds.ts`, `WORLDGEN_VERSION` in `config.ts`, plus their unit tests. Pure data layer, nothing wired in. Green: builds, tests pass, app still runs on the legacy save (no consumer yet).
2. **`feat(save): migrate the legacy single save into a default profile + world`** — `lib/game/legacyMigration.ts` + test. Still not called from UI. Green.
3. **`refactor(game): parameterize useMinecraftGame by world + profile`** — thread `worldId`/`profile` props through `MinecraftGame` and the hook; per-world `saveKey`, skin-from-profile, `persistGame` key arg. Temporarily, `app/page.tsx` constructs a single migrated world+profile and passes them in (a thin inline shim) so the app keeps booting straight into one world. Green: e2e helpers updated to seed a default profile/world.
4. **`feat(menu): add GameShell state machine and menu screens`** — `GameShell.tsx`, `components/menu/*`, menu CSS, run `migrateLegacySave` on boot, `key={worldId}` remount, `onQuitToWorlds`/`onReloadWorld`. `app/page.tsx` → `GameShell`. Rewrite `loadNow`/`resetNow` to nonce-remount; add `quitToWorlds`. Add the PauseMenu "Quit to Worlds" button. Component tests for menus + updated PauseMenu test. Green.
5. **`test(e2e): boot through the menu and cover world switching`** — update `e2e/helpers.ts` + `e2e/smoke.e2e.ts` key literals; add `e2e/menu.e2e.ts`. Green.
6. **`docs: document profiles, worlds, and the save-key model`** — `save-format.md`, `architecture.md`, `manual.md`, `reference.md`, `testing.md`, `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.

(If you want stricter atomicity, split commit 3 into "add params (default to legacy key)" then "switch consumer".)

---

- Whether any other module imports `SAVE_KEY` for non-migration use (grep showed only `useMinecraftGame.ts`); if so, point them at `worldSaveKey`.
- `app/layout.tsx` CSS import order if you add `app/menu.css` (add after `ui.css`).

A single localStorage\nkey (`minecraft_save_v7`) holds the only world; the player's appearance lives in a\nseparate global key (`minecraft_skin_v1`). The app boots straight into that one world —\nthere is no title/menu, no way to keep more than one save, and no notion of distinct\nplayers sharing a browser.\n\nThis feature adds **multiple named worlds** and **multiple player profiles**, with the\ndecided model: **a profile owns its worlds** (1 profile : N worlds), like user accounts\non a shared console. Selecting a profile filters the world list. A profile carries\n**name + skin only**; audio settings stay global. Shipped as **one combined feature** on\none branch.\n\nThe architecture is well-suited to this: the save layer's `readSave`/`writeSave` already\ntake a key parameter (always called with the one constant today), the engine is fully\nheadless and disposable, and the mount lifecycle already tears down everything cleanly on\nunmount — so worlds can be switched at runtime via a React `key` remount, with no page\nreload and no engine refactor.\n\nBranch: `feat/worlds-and-profiles` off `main`.\n\n## Data model (localStorage)\n\n| Key | Shape | Notes |\n|---|---|---|\n| `minecraft_profiles_v1` | `ProfilesManifest` | profile list + `activeProfileId` |\n| `minecraft_worlds_v1` | `WorldsManifest` | world index across all profiles |\n| `minecraft_world_save_<worldId>` | `SaveData` (v5, **unchanged**) | one bundled blob per world, via existing `readSave`/`writeSave` |\n| `minecraft_audio_v1` | `AudioSettings` | **global, untouched** |\n| `minecraft_skin_v1` | `SkinSettings` | legacy — read once during migration, then goes stale |\n| `minecraft_save_v7` | `SaveData` | legacy — consumed once during migration |\n\n```ts\n// lib/game/profiles.ts\ntype Profile = { id: string; name: string; skinId: SkinId; createdAt: number };\ntype ProfilesManifest = { version: 1; profiles: Profile[]; activeProfileId: string | null };\n\n// lib/game/worlds.ts\ntype WorldMeta = {\n  id: string; profileId: string;        // the 1:N link\n  name: string; seed: number;\n  worldgenVersion: number;              // 7 today (see below)\n  createdAt: number; lastPlayedAt: number;\n};\ntype WorldsManifest = { version: 1; worlds: WorldMeta[] };\nconst WORLD_SAVE_PREFIX = \"minecraft_world_save_\";\nfunction worldSaveKey(id: string) { return WORLD_SAVE_PREFIX + id; }\n```\n\nBoth manifests use a `{ version: 1, ... }` envelope so they can migrate later, mirroring\n`SaveData.version`. Readers are **total** (never throw): drop malformed entries, coerce\n`skinId` via `isSkinId`, and repair `activeProfileId` if it points at a deleted profile.\n\n**Seed:** resolved once at `createWorld(profileId, name, seedInput)` and stored on\n`WorldMeta.seed` (the manifest becomes the source of truth). Empty input → random in the\nsame `Math.floor(Math.random()*2147483647)` range the engine uses today; numeric input →\nclamped int; non-numeric text → stable hash (Minecraft-style). Store `WORLDGEN_VERSION` (= 7 today, new `config.ts` constant) on each\nworld. The historical `SAVE_KEY` bumps that invalidated block-diff baselines become a\nper-world check: if `world.worldgenVersion !== WORLDGEN_VERSION` at load, discard that\nworld's blob and reboot from its stored seed. If `minecraft_profiles_v1` exists → return (already migrated).\n2. Read legacy `minecraft_save_v7` (runs the v1→v5 chain) and `minecraft_skin_v1`.\n3. `createProfile(\"Player\", legacySkin)` → becomes active.\n4. If a legacy save exists: `createWorld(profile.id, \"My World\", String(seed))` then\n   **copy** the blob to `worldSaveKey(world.id)` (copy, not move — a quota failure can't\n   lose data), then `removeItem(\"minecraft_save_v7\")` after the copy succeeds.\n5. No legacy save (brand-new player): just the default profile, zero worlds.\n\nLeave `minecraft_skin_v1` in place (harmless; per-profile skin now owns appearance).\n\n## React shell / menu state machine\n\nSplit `app/page.tsx` so a new **menu shell** sits *above* `MinecraftGame` — the game\ncomponent stays \"the running game for one world\" and its big lifecycle `useEffect` is\nuntouched.\n\n- **New `components/GameShell.tsx`** owns the screen machine + active profile/world +\n  a `playNonce`, runs `migrateLegacySave()` once, and renders the game subtree.\n  ```ts\n  type Screen =\n    | { name: \"profile-select\" }\n    | { name: \"world-select\"; profileId: string }\n    | { name: \"play\"; profileId: string; worldId: string };\n  ```\n  Default landing: `profile-select` (active profile highlighted). Flows: pick profile →\n  world-select; pick world → `touchWorld(id)` then play; create/rename/delete profile &\n  world stay on their screens; \"Back\" world→profile.\n\n- **Runtime world switch with NO reload — the `key` trick.** Render play as:\n  ```tsx\n  <MinecraftGame\n    key={`${screen.worldId}:${playNonce}`}   // remounts → effect cleanup disposes old engine/renderer\n    worldId={screen.worldId} world={world} profile={activeProfile}\n    onQuitToWorlds={...} onReloadWorld={() => setPlayNonce(n => n + 1)}\n  />\n  ```\n  Changing `worldId` (or bumping `playNonce`) unmounts the subtree, which runs the existing\n  cleanup in `useMinecraftGame.ts:258-273` (disposes renderer/audio/input, clears rAF +\n  autosave interval, removes `beforeunload`). A fresh mount boots the new world. This is\n  the central move — **zero engine changes**.\n\n- **New menu components** under `components/menu/`: `ProfileSelect.tsx`,\n  `WorldSelect.tsx` (worlds for active profile, sorted by `lastPlayedAt`),\n  `CreateProfileForm.tsx` (name + skin swatch grid, reusing `SKIN_PRESETS` /\n  `skinPortraitUrl` exactly as `PauseMenu` does), `CreateWorldForm.tsx` (name + optional\n  seed). Style with existing tokens (`.mc-button`, `.pause-overlay`); add `.menu-screen`/\n  `.profile-card`/`.world-card` to a new `app/menu.css` imported after `ui.css` in\n  `app/layout.tsx`. Zero binary assets — portraits use the generated `skinPortraitUrl`.\n\n## Wiring `useMinecraftGame` to the selected world + profile\n\n`useMinecraftGame(opts: { worldId; world: WorldMeta; profile; onQuitToWorlds; onReloadWorld })`,\nthreaded through `MinecraftGame`. Capture `saveKeyRef = worldSaveKey(opts.worldId)` (stable\nfor the mount's life). this is \"Reset World\", not delete).\n- **Skin from profile**: replace `readSkinSettings()` (100) with `opts.profile.skinId`; `updateSkin` (116) calls `setProfileSkin(opts.profile.id, id)` instead of `writeSkinSettings`, still `setPlayerSkin(...)`.\n- **New `quitToWorlds()`**: `persistGame(engine, saveKeyRef.current, flashMessage)` *then* `opts.onQuitToWorlds()` — the autosave interval is cleared on unmount and `beforeunload` doesn't fire on in-app nav, so the quit must save synchronously.\n- **Audio unchanged** — stays global.\n\n`components/game/PauseMenu.tsx`: add a **\"Quit to Worlds\"** button (new `onQuitToWorlds`\nprop) next to the existing Save/Load/Reset; skin grid now edits the active profile.\n\n## Files\n\n**New:** `lib/game/manifest.ts` (versioned read/write envelope), `lib/game/profiles.ts`,\n`lib/game/worlds.ts`, `lib/game/legacyMigration.ts`, `components/GameShell.tsx`,\n`components/menu/{ProfileSelect,WorldSelect,CreateProfileForm,CreateWorldForm}.tsx`,\n`app/menu.css`.\n\n**Modified:** `app/page.tsx` (render `GameShell`), `app/layout.tsx` (import menu.css),\n`components/MinecraftGame.tsx` (accept/pass props), `lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts` (above),\n`components/game/PauseMenu.tsx` (Quit to Worlds), `lib/game/config.ts`\n(`WORLDGEN_VERSION`; mark `SAVE_KEY` as legacy/migration-only).\n\n**Reuse (don't reinvent):** `readSave`/`writeSave` (`lib/game/save.ts:75,92`),\n`GameEngine` `{ save, seed }` precedence (`GameEngine.ts:88`), `SKIN_PRESETS` /\n`isSkinId` / `skinPortraitUrl` (`playerSkins.ts`), the existing dispose-on-unmount cleanup.\n\n## Edge cases\n\n- **Delete active world:** only reachable from `world-select` (not while playing). On any world delete, also `removeItem(worldSaveKey(id))`.\n- **Delete profile with N worlds:** cascade — remove each world's blob + manifest entry *before* removing the profile.\n- **Last world / last profile gone:** empty world-select state (\"Create your first world\"); if zero profiles, route to \"create first profile\" (`activeProfileId = null`, no profile-less play).\n- **Autosave to the right key after a switch:** guaranteed — old interval is cleared in cleanup before the new mount installs its own reading the new `saveKeyRef`.\n- **Pointer lock:** menus are plain DOM with no canvas; the \"Double-click to play\" hint only renders inside `play`; cleanup already runs `exitPointerLock()` so the cursor is free in menus.\n- **StrictMode double-mount (dev):** migration is idempotent, so safe.\n\n## Testing & verification\n\n**Unit** (co-located `*.test.ts`, injectable `fakeStorage()` per `skinSettings.test.ts`):\n`profiles.test.ts` (CRUD, corrupt-manifest fallback, `activeProfileId` repair),\n`worlds.test.ts` (seed resolution: empty/numeric/text-hash/clamp; `worldsForProfile`\nfilter+sort; `worldSaveKey`; delete), `legacyMigration.test.ts` (all branches +\nidempotency + blob copied with matching seed + legacy key removed + audio untouched).\n\n**Component** (`*.test.tsx`): `ProfileSelect`/`WorldSelect`/`CreateWorldForm` (lists,\ncallbacks, empty states), extend `PauseMenu.test.tsx` (Quit to Worlds fires; add prop to\n`renderMenu` defaults), optional `GameShell.test.tsx` with a mocked `MinecraftGame`\nasserting the `key` changes on switch.\n\n**E2E** (`e2e/*.e2e.ts`): new `menu.e2e.ts` — fresh storage → profile-select → create\nprofile → create world (fixed seed) → play, assert `window.__monecraft` boots with that\nseed; pause → Quit to Worlds → re-enter, seed persists; create a 2nd world, switch, assert\n`__monecraft.engine.state.world.seed` changes **with no page navigation**. Update\n`e2e/helpers.ts` to pre-seed a default profile+world via `addInitScript` so existing\ngameplay tests still land in play. Manual pass: create two profiles, create worlds under each, switch worlds\nwithout reload, confirm autosave lands in the right blob, reset a world, delete a world and\na profile, confirm a pre-existing `minecraft_save_v7` migrates into \"Player / My World\".\n\n**Docs (ship with the change):** `docs/save-format.md` (manifests, per-world key, legacy\nmigration, `worldgenVersion` discard rule — primary), `docs/architecture.md` (GameShell →\n`key` remount lifecycle), `docs/manual.md` + `docs/reference.md` (menus, seed entry),\n`docs/testing.md` (e2e boots through menus), `README.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`.\n\n## Commit slicing (Conventional Commits; lint/build green each step)\n\n1. `feat(save): add versioned profile + world manifests` — `manifest/profiles/worlds.ts`, `WORLDGEN_VERSION`, unit tests.
