docs/adding-content.md:87:- **Any block write changes the deterministic output**, so this breaks the worldgen hash. Re-baseline the `generation.test.ts` digests and bump `SAVE_KEY` per the [testing.md](testing.md) policy; add a structural probe test (count your blocks, assert they generate) that survives the re-baseline.
docs/tuning.md:182:`AUTOSAVE_INTERVAL_MS`, `SAVE_KEY`, `STUCK_RESET_SECONDS`, `RENDER_RADIUS`,
docs/tuning.md:195:- **`SAVE_KEY`** (`"minecraft_save_v6"`) is the localStorage key name. Note it's
docs/tuning.md:197:  [save-format.md](save-format.md)) — renaming `SAVE_KEY` orphans every existing
docs/architecture.md:81:- **Skins**: the body is colored by a `PlayerPalette` from `lib/game/playerSkins.ts` (six presets); `GameRenderer.setPlayerSkin(palette)` recolors the named materials in place. The choice persists on the active **profile** (`lib/game/profiles.ts`; `updateSkin` calls `setProfileSkin`) — identity, not world state — and both the create-profile and pause-menu pickers' bust portraits (`lib/ui/skinPortrait.ts`) derive from the same palettes. (The legacy global `minecraft_skin_v1` key, still defined in `lib/game/skinSettings.ts`, is read only once by the save migration to seed the default profile's skin.)
docs/architecture.md:106:- `config.ts` — every gameplay tunable, named: physics, hunger rules, daylight thresholds, mob director, mining reach, autosave interval, the legacy `SAVE_KEY`, and `WORLDGEN_VERSION` (the worldgen baseline each world records).
docs/testing.md:65:The **deep-cave lava worldgen** is the most recent legitimate re-baseline (case 1): the four worldgen digests and the in-region meshing snapshot were re-rolled and `SAVE_KEY` bumped v6 → v7. As with dungeons the structural probes (surface heights/biomes/snow/water) were left unchanged on purpose — `placeLava` runs after `placeOres` so it can't shift the ore→tree RNG stream, and the lava sits at or below `lavaLevel`, so the surface is byte-identical. A probe asserts lava is present and never rises above `lavaLevel`. Note the per-voxel **lighting** change re-rolled nothing: light is a derived runtime cache, never part of the generated bytes, and the meshing snapshot hashes only vertex positions — the new `aLight` attribute leaves them untouched.
docs/testing.md:67:The **0.7.0 dungeon worldgen** was the prior re-baseline (case 1): the four worldgen digests and the in-region meshing snapshot were re-rolled and `SAVE_KEY` bumped v5 → v6. The structural probes were left unchanged — dungeons are wholly underground, so the surface bytes are identical.
docs/save-format.md:18:Each world's `SaveData` is stored in its own localStorage key, `minecraft_world_save_<worldId>` (`worldSaveKey` in `lib/game/worlds.ts`). Read/write and restore validation live in `lib/game/save.ts` — `readSave`/`writeSave` take the key as a parameter and the `Storage` is injectable for tests; `GameEngine.serialize()` produces the save from live state, and `lib/game/engine/blockChanges.ts` maintains the block diff. (The legacy single-world key `SAVE_KEY` = `minecraft_save_v7` is now read only once, by the migration described under [Profiles & worlds](#profiles--worlds).)
docs/save-format.md:30:- **v5** (unchanged for caves & lighting) — the lighting system adds no persisted state (light is a derived cache, recomputed at load), and lava is worldgen, not a saved field. So the save _schema_ stays at version 5. But the deep-cave lava changes the deterministic terrain baseline, so **`SAVE_KEY` is bumped `minecraft_save_v6` → `minecraft_save_v7`**: old block-diffs were computed against the lava-free world and must never be applied to the new one. Pre-lava saves are discarded in practice. Torches are additive items/blocks, persisted by string id / in the block diff like any other placed block.
docs/save-format.md:31:- **v5** (unchanged for ranged combat & the endgame boss) — bows, arrows, the Cursed Totem, the Dragon Heart, and the Dragon Sword are **additive items**, persisted by string id in the existing inventory/container slots, so no schema change was needed. **Arrows in flight, the boss, its minions, and the `victory` flag are all transient** (never serialized — like mobs), and there is no worldgen change (the boss is summoned, not generated), so neither the `version` nor `SAVE_KEY` moved.
docs/save-format.md:32:- **v5** — adds optional `lootedChests` (opened/broken dungeon loot chests). `migrateSaveV4toV5` is a pure version bump (absent → no dungeon chests yet looted). Unlike v3/v4, **`SAVE_KEY` is bumped to `minecraft_save_v6`** here — not because of the save _shape_ (the field is additive) but because the dungeon worldgen changed the deterministic terrain baseline, so old block-diffs would index against the wrong world. Pre-dungeon saves are therefore discarded in practice; the migration keeps the version chain complete regardless. `readSave` chains v1 → v2 → v3 → v4 → v5.
docs/save-format.md:33:- **v4** — adds optional `blockEntities` (chest contents). `migrateSaveV3toV4` is a pure version bump (absent → restores as no containers), so **`SAVE_KEY` was unchanged** at the time (player-placed chests only; worldgen and the index formula were untouched) and v1/v2/v3 saves load fine.
docs/save-format.md:34:- **v3** — adds optional `dayClock`, `hearts`, `hunger`, and `spawnPoint`. `migrateSaveV2toV3` is a pure version bump (the new fields stay absent and restore as engine defaults), so **`SAVE_KEY` is unchanged** and v1/v2 saves load fine.
docs/save-format.md:43:- **`minecraft_profiles_v1`** (`lib/game/profiles.ts`) — the profile list plus `activeProfileId`. A profile is `{ id, name, skinId, createdAt }`: player identity only (name + skin). The skin moved here from the old global `minecraft_skin_v1` key; a dangling `activeProfileId` is repaired on read.
docs/save-format.md:47:`worldgenVersion` records the `WORLDGEN_VERSION` (`lib/game/config.ts`, currently 7) the world was generated under. This **replaces the old whole-store `SAVE_KEY` bump**: when the worldgen baseline changes, bump `WORLDGEN_VERSION`, and a world whose recorded version differs has its stale block-diffs discarded and reboots from its stored seed — per-world, with no key rename and without touching unaffected worlds.
docs/save-format.md:53:`migrateLegacySave` (`lib/game/legacyMigration.ts`) runs once on first boot, gated on the **absence** of `minecraft_profiles_v1` (its presence is the already-migrated flag, which survives a rollback that re-creates the legacy key). It creates a default "Player" profile carrying the old global skin, and — if a legacy `minecraft_save_v7` exists — **copies** it into one "My World" blob, then removes the legacy key. The copy precedes the delete so a mid-migration failure can never lose the original.
```

185	`RENDER_RADIUS` is the biggest **performance** lever: the renderer meshes one region

187	`RENDER_GRID` is how far the player moves before that mesh rebuilds (smaller = more

84	## A worldgen structure (houses, dungeons, …)
85	
86	- Structures are placed in `lib/world/generation.ts` at the end of the `generateWorld` pipeline. Mirror `placeHouse`/`placeDungeons`: loop a `GEN.<thing>Count`, sample positions, validate, and write blocks with `world.set`. Append your pass **last** so later passes don't overwrite it, and add the count to the frozen `GEN` object.
87	- **Any block write changes the deterministic output**, so this breaks the worldgen hash. Re-baseline the `generation.test.ts` digests and bump `SAVE_KEY` per the [testing.md](testing.md) policy; add a structural probe test (count your blocks, assert they generate) that survives the re-baseline.
88	- If the structure needs its positions known at runtime (dungeons re-derive chest/spawner indices on load), expose a **derive pass** like `collectDungeonSites`: factor placement into a shared routine that either writes blocks or records indices, drawing from a **dedicated PRNG seeded only from `world.seed`** (not the shared gen stream) with a fixed number of draws per structure, and validate against **seed-pure** terrain (`terrainTopY`, `getBiome`) — never `highestSolidY`, which a cave or player edit can shift out from under the derive pass.

> AGENT

All 13 e2e tests pass. The feature is complete and the full verify gate is green. Here's the summary.

## Multiple Worlds & Player Profiles — done

Built on branch `feat/worlds-and-profiles` as the model you chose: **profiles own worlds** (name + skin per profile, audio stays global), shipped as one feature in six focused commits.

```
7b60826 feat(save): add versioned profile and world manifests
24a9e63 feat(save): migrate the legacy single save into a default profile + world
3fea321 refactor(game): drive the game by a selected world and profile
92342a6 feat(menu): add profile and world selection menus
2f6e3f3 test(e2e): cover the menu flow and in-app world switching
278a5b4 docs: document profiles, worlds, and the per-world save model
```

**What it does**
- Opens to a **profile-select → world-select → play** menu (create / rename / delete on both levels, optional seed entry).
- Each profile keeps its own worlds; each world saves to its own `minecraft_world_save_<id>` key. Skin moved onto the profile; audio stays global.
- Switching worlds / Load / Reset remounts the game via a React `key` — old engine/renderer disposed, new one booted, **no page reload**. The tab remembers its world in `sessionStorage`, so a reload resumes straight back into play.
- A one-time migration folds any existing single-world save into a default "Player" profile + "My World" (copy-before-delete, idempotent).
- New per-world `WORLDGEN_VERSION` replaces the old whole-store `SAVE_KEY` bump: a future worldgen change discards only affected worlds' stale diffs.

**Verification (all green):** `lint`, `typecheck`, `format:check`, `build`, **494 unit/component tests**, **13 Playwright e2e** (10 gameplay through the menu + 3 menu/world-switch). Docs updated across save-format, architecture, testing, tuning, adding-content, manual, README, and CHANGELOG.

Nothing has been pushed — the branch is local for you to review (matching your usual flow). When you want, I can open a PR against `hutusi/main`.

One product note worth surfacing: a brand-new install auto-creates a "Player" profile, so first-run lands on profile-select with one profile rather than an empty list — I judged that smoother than an empty screen, but it's a one-line change in `GameShell`/migration if you'd prefer the empty "create your first profile" state.

> DEVELOPER

agree

> DEVELOPER

is it better to let the user to choose a profile with name, skin and others? what do you think?

> AGENT

Good question, and it's really two questions. Let me separate them.

## Can users already choose name + skin? Yes — except on first run

Creating a profile *already* prompts for **name + skin** (`CreateProfileForm` — text field + the 6-swatch picker). So users do choose. The one gap is the **first-run auto-create**: a brand-new player gets a pre-made "Player" profile with the default skin instead of being asked. That's the thing actually worth changing.

My recommendation: on a truly fresh install (no legacy save, no profiles), drop the player **straight into the create-profile form** so their very first interaction is "who are you / pick a look," then into their empty world list. Returning players and the migrated legacy save are untouched. It's a small, localized change (the first-run branch in migration + `GameShell`) and it makes the opening feel personal instead of handing them a generic "Player."

## Should a profile carry "others" (more than name + skin)? I'd hold the line at name + skin

Here's my honest take on why *not* to pile more on:

- **A profile's job is identity + world ownership** on a shared browser. Name labels it; skin is the visual identity. That's complete for the job.
- **Most "other" things belong elsewhere.** Difficulty / game-mode is per-*world* in Minecraft (different worlds, different rules) — not per-profile. Playtime/achievements are naturally per-world and can be aggregated later without living on the profile record. Audio you already decided stays global.
- **More fields = more persistence surface** (migration, validation, UI) for little payoff. The manifests stay lean and the readers stay simple.

If the real itch is "I want *more* customization choice," the high-value place to spend it is the **skin system itself** — e.g. a custom-color option beyond the 6 presets. That's better than profile fields because it automatically shows up in *both* the create-profile form and the in-game pause picker (they share `SKIN_PRESETS` / `skinPortraitUrl`), and it's a pure `lib/game/playerSkins.ts` extension with zero save-format churn.

So, concretely, two things I'd actually do, in order of value:

1. **First-run create-profile flow** (small, clear win) — replace the silent "Player" with a "set up your profile" step.
2. **Richer skins later** (optional) — a custom-color picker if you want more expression, built into the skin system so it benefits the whole game.

Want me to do #1 now? And if richer skins appeal, I can scope #2 separately — but I'd keep the profile record itself at name + skin.

> DEVELOPER

I think we can take both, what do you think?

```json
{
  "command": "gh pr create --repo hutusi/monecraft --base main --head feat/worlds-and-profiles --title \"feat: multiple worlds and player profiles\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n\nAdds **multiple named worlds** under **multiple player profiles** — like user accounts on a shared console. The game now opens to a menu (profile → world → play) instead of dropping straight into one fixed world. All client-side in localStorage; **zero new assets** (the menus reuse the existing Minecraft-style chrome and generated skin portraits).\n\n**Model:** a profile owns its worlds (1 profile : N worlds). A profile carries identity only — **name + skin**; audio settings stay global.\n\n## What changed\n\n- **Two-level save hierarchy** (`lib/game/profiles.ts`, `lib/game/worlds.ts`): a versioned `minecraft_profiles_v1` manifest and a `minecraft_worlds_v1` index, with each world's `SaveData` in its own `minecraft_world_save_<id>` key (via the existing `readSave`/`writeSave`, which already took a key param). Readers are total — malformed entries dropped, dangling active-profile repaired. New-world seeds resolve once at creation (blank → random, numeric → clamped, other text → stable FNV hash).\n- **Menu shell** (`components/GameShell.tsx`, `components/menu/*`): profile-select → world-select → play state machine with create / rename / delete on both levels (deleting a profile cascades its worlds and their save blobs). Switching worlds, Load, or Reset remounts the game subtree via a React `key`, so the engine/renderer are disposed and re-created **without a page reload**. The tab remembers its active world in `sessionStorage`, so a reload resumes straight back into play while a fresh tab cold-starts at the menu. The pause menu gains **Save & Quit to Worlds**.\n- **Per-world worldgen versioning** (`WORLDGEN_VERSION` in `config.ts`): each world records the baseline it was generated under. A future worldgen change bumps that constant and discards only the affected worlds' stale block-diffs — replacing the old whole-store `SAVE_KEY` rename, which reset every world. The save schema is unchanged (still version 5).\n- **One-time migration** (`lib/game/legacyMigration.ts`): an existing single-world save is folded into a default \"Player\" profile (carrying the old skin) plus one \"My World\"; the legacy blob is copied before the old key is removed, so a failure can't lose data. Idempotent (gated on the profiles manifest's absence).\n- The player skin moved off the old global `minecraft_skin_v1` key onto the profile (`updateSkin` → `setProfileSkin`).\n\n## Save-format / worldgen impact\n\n- New localStorage keys: `minecraft_profiles_v1`, `minecraft_worlds_v1`, `minecraft_world_save_<id>`. The legacy `minecraft_save_v7` is read once by the migration, then removed. **Existing worlds migrate automatically** into \"Player / My World\" — no data loss.\n- No worldgen change; `WORLDGEN_VERSION` is introduced at the current baseline (7). Save schema stays v5.\n\n## Tests\n\n- **Unit:** `profiles.test.ts`, `worlds.test.ts`, `legacyMigration.test.ts` (manifest CRUD, total readers, seed resolution, ordering, cascade delete, copy-then-remove migration + idempotency, audio untouched).\n- **Component:** `ProfileSelect` / `WorldSelect` / `CreateWorldForm` tests; `PauseMenu` test extended for Save & Quit to Worlds.\n- **E2E:** new `e2e/menu.e2e.ts` (create a world with a fixed seed and play it; quit then switch worlds with no page reload; reload resumes the tab's world; a second profile gets its own world list). The smoke fixture seeds a profile + world and enters via the menu, so the existing gameplay suite passes unchanged.\n\nFull gate green: `lint`, `typecheck`, `format:check`, `build`, 494 unit/component tests, 13 Playwright e2e.\n\n## Docs\n\nUpdated `save-format.md` (Profiles & worlds + migration), `architecture.md` (menu shell + key-remount lifecycle, per-profile skin), `testing.md` (new coverage rows), `tuning.md` + `adding-content.md` (`WORLDGEN_VERSION` replaces the `SAVE_KEY` bump), `README.md` + `manual.md` (player flow), and `CHANGELOG.md`.\n\n## Open follow-up (not in this PR)\n\nFirst run currently auto-creates a default \"Player\" profile. A nice onboarding tweak would be to instead drop a brand-new player straight into the create-profile form (name + skin). Left out pending a decision.\nEOF\n)\" 2>&1 | tail -5",
  "description": "Open PR against hutusi/main"
}

1	import { expect, test } from "@playwright/test";

3	/**
4	 * Profile/world menu flow. Unlike the gameplay smoke suite (which seeds a world
5	 * and enters it via a fixture), these start from empty storage and drive the
6	 * real menus. A fresh install runs the legacy migration, which seeds a default
7	 * "Player" profile with no worlds.
8	 */
9	
10	test("create worlds, play them, and switch between them without a reload", async ({ page }) => {
11	  await page.goto("/");
12	
13	  // Enter the migration-created default profile.
14	  await page.getByText("Player", { exact: true }).click();
15	  await expect(page.getByText(/No worlds yet/i)).toBeVisible();
16	
17	  // Create the first world with a fixed seed and play it.
18	  await page.getByTestId("new-world").click();
19	  await page.getByLabel("World name").fill("Alpha");
20	  await page.getByLabel("World seed").fill("100");
21	  await page.getByRole("button", { name: "Create World" }).click();
22	
23	  await page.waitForFunction(() => window.__monecraft !== undefined, undefined, { timeout: 30000 });
24	  expect(await page.evaluate(() => window.__monecraft!.engine.state.world.seed)).toBe(100);
25	
26	  // A marker on window survives a React remount but not a real page navigation,
27	  // so it proves the upcoming world switch happens in-app with no reload.
28	  await page.evaluate(() => ((window as unknown as { __noReload?: boolean }).__noReload = true));
29	
30	  // Quit back to the world list and create a second world with a different seed.
31	  await page.keyboard.press("Escape");
32	  await page.getByRole("button", { name: "Save & Quit to Worlds" }).click();
33	  await expect(page.getByText("Alpha")).toBeVisible();
34	
35	  await page.getByTestId("new-world").click();
36	  await page.getByLabel("World name").fill("Beta");
37	  await page.getByLabel("World seed").fill("200");
38	  await page.getByRole("button", { name: "Create World" }).click();
39	
40	  await page.waitForFunction(() => window.__monecraft?.engine.state.world.seed === 200, undefined, { timeout: 30000 });
41	  expect(await page.evaluate(() => (window as unknown as { __noReload?: boolean }).__noReload)).toBe(true);
42	});
43	
44	test("reloading resumes the world being played", async ({ page }) => {
45	  await page.goto("/");
46	  await page.getByText("Player", { exact: true }).click();
47	  await page.getByTestId("new-world").click();
48	  await page.getByLabel("World name").fill("Persistent");
49	  await page.getByLabel("World seed").fill("777");
50	  await page.getByRole("button", { name: "Create World" }).click();
51	  await page.waitForFunction(() => window.__monecraft?.engine.state.world.seed === 777, undefined, { timeout: 30000 });
52	
53	  await page.reload();
54	  // No menu click needed — the tab resumes straight back into the same world.

56	});

## The improvement — decided & built (local, not pushed)
Per your choices:
- **First run → create-profile form.** A brand-new player now opens straight into "Create Your Profile" (name + skin) instead of getting a pre-made "Player". This also covers the delete-last-profile case (same forced-create screen, no Cancel).
- **Profile scope stays name + skin.** No "others" added — difficulty/mode is per-world, and richer expression (if you ever want it) belongs in the skin system, not the profile record.

```
1	# Multiple Worlds & Multiple Player Profiles
2	
3	## Context
4	
5	Today the game has exactly one world and one implicit player. A single localStorage
6	key (`minecraft_save_v7`) holds the only world; the player's appearance lives in a
7	separate global key (`minecraft_skin_v1`). The app boots straight into that one world —
8	there is no title/menu, no way to keep more than one save, and no notion of distinct
9	players sharing a browser.
10	
11	This feature adds **multiple named worlds** and **multiple player profiles**, with the
12	decided model: **a profile owns its worlds** (1 profile : N worlds), like user accounts
13	on a shared console. Selecting a profile filters the world list. A profile carries
14	**name + skin only**; audio settings stay global. Shipped as **one combined feature** on
15	one branch.
16	
17	The architecture is well-suited to this: the save layer's `readSave`/`writeSave` already
18	take a key parameter (always called with the one constant today), the engine is fully
19	headless and disposable, and the mount lifecycle already tears down everything cleanly on
20	unmount — so worlds can be switched at runtime via a React `key` remount, with no page
```

Perfect. Now I have all the information needed. Let me compile a comprehensive report with exact file paths, line numbers, and signatures.

## Complete World-Creation Seam Report

### 1. **CreateWorldForm.tsx** — Full form state and props

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/components/menu/CreateWorldForm.tsx`

**Props signature (lines 4-7):**
```typescript
type CreateWorldFormProps = {
  onCreate: (name: string, seed: string) => void;  // line 5
  onCancel: () => void;
};
```

**State (lines 11-12):**
- `name` (string)
- `seed` (string)

**Form structure (lines 14-55):**
- Two text inputs: name (line 24-33, `MAX_WORLD_NAME` constraint) and seed (line 37-44)
- Submit calls `onCreate(name, seed)` with raw string values (line 19)
- Uses `.menu-form`, `.menu-field`, `.menu-input`, `.menu-form-actions` classes (lines 16, 22, 25, 46)

---

### 2. **WorldSelect.tsx** — Form rendering and flow

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/components/menu/WorldSelect.tsx`

**Creation flow (lines 22-34):**
```typescript
if (creating) {
  return (
    <MenuScreen title={`${profile.name} — New World`}>
      <CreateWorldForm
        onCreate={(name, seed) => {  // line 26
          const world = createWorld(profile.id, name, seed);  // line 27
          setCreating(false);
          onPlay(world.id);  // line 29 — straight into the freshly created world
        }}
        onCancel={() => setCreating(false)}
      />
    </MenuScreen>
  );
}
```

The form is rendered only when `creating` state is true. On `onCreate`, it calls `createWorld(profile.id, name, seed)` (3 args, no deps object passed), then immediately calls `onPlay(world.id)`.

---

### 3. **worlds.ts** — Signatures, WorldMeta, resolveSeed

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/worlds.ts`

**WorldMeta type (lines 17-25):**
```typescript
export type WorldMeta = {
  id: string;
  profileId: string;
  name: string;
  seed: number;  // <-- resolved integer
  worldgenVersion: number;  // <-- WORLDGEN_VERSION at creation
  createdAt: number;
  lastPlayedAt: number;
};
```

**createWorld signature (line 106):**
```typescript
export function createWorld(
  profileId: string, 
  name: string, 
  seedInput: string | null, 
  deps: ManifestDeps & { rng?: () => number } = {}
): WorldMeta
```
- Returns a fully formed `WorldMeta` with resolved seed and timestamps
- `seedInput` is string | null (from form, typically string)
- `deps` is optional; WorldSelect passes none

**resolveSeed signature (line 60):**
```typescript
export function resolveSeed(
  seedInput: string | null, 
  rng: () => number = Math.random
): number
```
- Blank → random (line 62: `Math.floor(rng() * MAX_SEED)`)
- Numeric text → clamped int (line 64: `Math.floor(Math.abs(asNumber)) % MAX_SEED`)
- Non-numeric text → stable FNV-1a hash via `hashStringToSeed()` (line 65)

**hashStringToSeed (lines 45-53):** FNV-1a hash, returns number in 0..MAX_SEED-1

---

### 4. **useMinecraftGame.ts and GameEngine.ts** — World boot seam

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts`

**Engine initialization (lines 144-152):**
```typescript
const attachMount = useCallback((node: HTMLDivElement | null) => {
  if (!node) {
    setCtx(null);
    return;
  }
  // A saved blob carries its own seed (engine prefers it); a fresh world (no
  // blob yet) boots from the world's stored seed.
  setCtx({ 
    engine: new GameEngine({ 
      save: readSave(saveKeyRef.current), 
      seed: worldSeedRef.current  // <-- from WorldMeta.seed
    }), 
    node 
  });
}, []);
```

**WorldMeta flow (lines 96-97):**
- `saveKeyRef` derived from `opts.world.id` → `worldSaveKey(opts.world.id)`
- `worldSeedRef` = `opts.world.seed` (the resolved integer)
- Only `seed` and optional `save` blob flow into engine

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts`

**GameEngineOptions signature (lines 55-64):**
```typescript
export type GameEngineOptions = {
  /** A parsed save to restore, or null for a fresh world. */
  save?: SaveData | null;
  /** Seed for a fresh world; ignored when a save is provided. */
  seed?: number;
  /** Randomness source for mob spawning/AI — injectable for deterministic tests. */
  rng?: () => number;
  /** World dimensions override for fast headless tests. */
  worldSize?: { x: number; y: number; z: number };
};
```

**Engine constructor (lines 84-91):**
```typescript
constructor(options: GameEngineOptions = {}) {
  const save = options.save ?? null;
  this.rng = options.rng ?? Math.random;

  const seed = save?.seed ?? options.seed ?? Math.floor(Math.random() * 2147483647);
  const size = options.worldSize ?? { x: WORLD_SIZE_X, y: WORLD_SIZE_Y, z: WORLD_SIZE_Z };
  const world = new VoxelWorld(size.x, size.y, size.z, seed);
  generateWorld(world);
```

**Only `seed` flows from WorldMeta into worldgen.** The `VoxelWorld` constructor (line 90) takes only `(sizeX, sizeY, sizeZ, seed)`. To pass a world-type param, you'd need to:
1. Add a field to `WorldMeta` (e.g., `worldType: string`)
2. Extend `GameEngineOptions` to accept it
3. Pass it through the constructor chain to `generateWorld()` and/or `VoxelWorld`

---

### 5. **types.ts** — SaveData shape

**File:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/game/types.ts`

**SaveData type (lines 115-118):**
```typescript
export type SaveData = Omit<SaveDataV4, "version"> & {
  version: 5;
  lootedChests?: number[];
};
```

**Full field set (read back through v4, v3, v2, v1):**
- `version: 5`
- `seed: number` (line 71 in SaveDataV1)
- `changes: Array<[number, number]>` (line 72)
- `inventorySlots?: SavedSlot[]` (line 74)
- `equippedArmor?: Partial<EquippedArmor>` (line 75)
- `selectedSlot: number` (line 76)
- `player: { x, y, z }` (line 77)
- `dayClock?: number` (line 90 in SaveDataV3)
- `hearts?: number` (line 91)
- `hunger?: number` (line 92)
- `spawnPoint?: { x, y, z } | null` (line 93)
- `blockEntities?: SavedContainer[]` (line 103 in SaveDataV4)
- `lootedChests?: number[]` (line 117)

## Summary for Threading a World-Type Param

To add world presets, thread a new field through this seam:

1. **Form** (`CreateWorldForm.tsx`): Add state for preset choice, pass to `onCreate(name, seed, preset?)`
2. **Chooser** (`WorldSelect.tsx`): Pass the preset through to `createWorld(profileId, name, seed, preset)`
3. **Persist** (`worlds.ts`): Add `worldType?: string` to `WorldMeta`, resolve in `createWorld()`, store in manifest
4. **Engine** (`GameEngine.ts`): Add `worldType?` to `GameEngineOptions`, pass to `generateWorld(world, worldType)` or equivalent
5. **Tests**: Assert the field flows end-to-end

The **CSS for the swatch grid is already there** — `.pause-skins-grid` and `.skin-swatch` styles can wrap your preset buttons, and `.menu-form` / `.menu-field` structure already work.

Perfect. Now let me create a comprehensive summary with specific findings:

## Comprehensive World Generation Analysis

I've traced the code thoroughly. Here's a concrete answer to your questions:

---

### 1. WORLD SIZE & SHAPE

**Fixed finite world:**
- **Dimensions:** `512 x 150 x 512` (X x Y x Z)
- **Files:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/blocks.ts` lines 1-3
  ```typescript
  export const WORLD_SIZE_X = 512;
  export const WORLD_SIZE_Y = 150;
  export const WORLD_SIZE_Z = 512;
  ```

Every world is **exactly the same size**—a single finite region. It's not chunked or infinite. All five biomes coexist within this bounded map, placed deterministically by the noise functions. The borders are walled off with bedrock at y=1..14 (line 20 in generation.ts: `borderWallHeight: 14`).

---

### 2. HOW THE SEED DRIVES GENERATION

**The seed modulates four independent noise dimensions:**

**A. Biome selection** (voxelWorld.ts lines 78–94):
- The seed is scaled by `0.007` and fed into **four independent sine-wave pairs**:
  - `temp`: temperature axis (controls desert vs. forest)
  - `moisture`: moisture axis (controls forest vs. plains)
  - `continental`: continentality axis (controls ocean vs. land)
  - `ridge`: ridge axis (controls mountains)
- Biome thresholds are **hardcoded**:
  ```typescript
  if (continental < -0.52) return Ocean;
  if ((continental > 0.5 && ridge > 0) || ridge > 0.8) return Mountains;
  if (temp > 0.2 && moisture < -0.12) return Desert;
  if (moisture > 0.18) return Forest;
  return Plains;  // default
  ```
- **Critical:** Every seed produces the **same biome distribution pattern across all five biomes** because the thresholds are fixed. A different seed shifts the wave phases, but all seeds have guaranteed Forest coverage >10% and each biome >2% (verified in the tests at generation.test.ts lines 123–127).

**B. Terrain height per biome** (generation.ts lines 94–134):
- Uses `smoothNoise2D(x, z, world.seed)` which adds the seed into a complex multi-octave sine mixture
- Terrain height = `baseHeight + noise * noiseScale` (clamped to y=5..145)
- **Per-biome baselines and amplitudes** (generation.ts lines 18–27):
  ```typescript
  Plains:    { baseHeight: 47, noiseScale: 2.0, ... }
  Desert:    { baseHeight: 46, noiseScale: 3.5, ... }
  Ocean:     { baseHeight: 30, noiseScale: 2.5, ... }
  Forest:    { baseHeight: 50, noiseScale: 4.0, ... }
  Mountains: { baseHeight: 55, noiseScale: 18.0, ... }
  ```
- **Mountains are always tallest** (amplitude 18×), oceans are always lowest (30 base), plains/desert/forest are intermediate.
- Different seeds produce **different height noise patterns** but the overall landscape character (mountain vs. ocean biome always exists at predictable Y ranges) is deterministic from the biome map.

**C. Caves** (generation.ts lines 149–198):
- 230 main caves + 150 chambers, all positioned by RNG seeded from `world.seed`
- Higher amplitude of seed perturbs cave frequency but doesn't change the total count

**D. Ores, houses, dungeons, trees, cacti** (all RNG-driven from the same PRNG):
- Same seed means identical cave locations, ore clusters, house placements, dungeon rooms, and tree positions

**Verdict on seed variety:**
- A seed **changes the fine detail** (terrain noise, cave/ore placement, tree positions) but **all seeds produce all biomes in roughly the same mix** (Ocean ~5–15%, Mountains ~5–10%, Desert ~5–10%, Forest ~15–20%, Plains ~50–60%). 
- The **landscape character is largely fixed**: sea level is always 43, mountain snow-cap is always >68, lava only spawns y≤9. So every world feels like the same basic template in different seed arrangements.

---

### 3. THE `GEN` CONSTANTS OBJECT

**Location:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/generation.ts` lines 18–50

```typescript
export const GEN = Object.freeze({
  seaLevel: 43,                          // Fixed water level
  borderWallHeight: 14,                  // Bedrock border height
  biomes: {
    [BiomeId.Plains]:    { baseHeight: 47, noiseScale: 2.0, treeChance: 0.18 },
    [BiomeId.Desert]:    { baseHeight: 46, noiseScale: 3.5, treeChance: 0.01 },
    [BiomeId.Ocean]:     { baseHeight: 30, noiseScale: 2.5, treeChance: 0 },
    [BiomeId.Forest]:    { baseHeight: 50, noiseScale: 4.0, treeChance: 0.6 },
    [BiomeId.Mountains]: { baseHeight: 55, noiseScale: 18.0, treeChance: 0.05 }
  },
  mountainStoneAboveY: 65,               // Mountains turn to stone above this
  snowAboveY: 68,                        // Snow caps mountains above this
  lavaLevel: 9,                          // Lava only at y ≤ 9
  beachMaxAboveSea: 1,
  beachDepthBelowSea: 2,
  caveCount: 230,                        // Numbe of cave tunnels
  caveChamberCount: 150,                 // Number of cave chambers
  treeAttempts: 9000,                    // Placement attempts (gated by biome chance)
  cactusChance: 0.012,                   // Per-column hash-based chance
  houseCount: 120,                       // Houses placed at random
  dungeonCount: 28,                      // Underground dungeons
  oreConfigs: [                          // Ore placement (5 types)
    { id: SliverOre,   attempts: 120000, minY: 3,  maxYOffset: 10, minSize: 3, maxSize: 10 },
    { id: RubyOre,     attempts: 52000,  minY: 2,  maxYOffset: 16, minSize: 3, maxSize: 10 },
    { id: GoldOre,     attempts: 36000,  minY: 2,  maxYOffset: 22, minSize: 3, maxSize: 10 },
    { id: SapphireOre, attempts: 28000,  minY: 2,  maxYOffset: 28, minSize: 2, maxSize: 7 },
    { id: DiamondOre,  attempts: 18000,  minY: 2,  maxYOffset: 36, minSize: 2, maxSize: 6 }
  ]
});
```

**Cheap vs. expensive parameterization:**

| Tunable | Impact | Cost | Save-format risk |
|---------|--------|------|------------------|
| **seaLevel** | Changes all water, beaches, ore Y ranges. Huge visual + gameplay impact. | Cheap (1 number) | HIGH—shifts entire ore distribution, breaks existing saves |
| **biome baseHeight / noiseScale** | Changes terrain amplitude per biome. Very visible. | Cheap (1 number per biome) | HIGH—shifts height noise RNG stream |
| **mountainStoneAboveY / snowAboveY** | Changes mountain appearance only. | Cheap | MEDIUM—visual only, no RNG impact |
| **lavaLevel** | Changes deep-cave hazards. | Cheap | MEDIUM—lavaLevel is tested (generation.test.ts line 101), but not RNG-critical |
| **caveCount / caveChamberCount** | Changes cave frequency. Very visible. | Cheap | HIGH—shifts RNG stream if changed |
| **treeAttempts** | Changes tree density. | Cheap | HIGH—shifts RNG stream |
| **cactusChance** | Desert cactus density. | Cheap | LOW—uses hash2D not rand() |
| **houseCount / dungeonCount** | Structure density. | Cheap | HIGH—shifts RNG stream |
| **oreConfigs** | Ore frequency & depth distribution. | Cheap | HIGH—shifts RNG stream |

**Best candidates for "world type" presets (safe to add if they fork the generation path early):**

1. **Superflat** (seaLevel=50, all baseHeight=50, all noiseScale=0.5) → flat plains
2. **Amplified** (all noiseScale ×2) → extreme terrain
3. **Islands** (seaLevel=100, all baseHeight=40) → mostly water, island peaks
4. **Lush** (Forest biome threshold: `moisture > 0.1` instead of `0.18`) → forest-heavy
5. **Deep caves** (caveCount=400, caveChamberCount=300) → cave-heavy
6. **Sparse** (houseCount=30, dungeonCount=10, treeAttempts=3000) → less cluttered

---

### 4. DETERMINISM & HASH CONTRACT

**Location:** `/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/generation.test.ts`

**The contract:**
```typescript
// Lines 13–24 of generation.ts (in the module header comment):
/**
 * Saves store only the seed plus block-change deltas, so generateWorld() must
 * produce byte-identical output for a given seed forever. Generation order, the
 * PRNG, the noise functions, and every constant below are part of the save
 * format. generation.test.ts pins the output with SHA-256 digests — if it fails
 * after a change here, the change breaks existing saves.
 */
```

**The pinning tests** (generation.test.ts lines 44–57):
```typescript
test.each([
  [1337, "2037297fd8b8269fa984f907b84bcf3b185e4c261ae4a93b089de0178cc510d2"],
  [1, "c55a0b3395d2a95643ded6af66d49bbea4de44d045f5a812f625f397f5b377d7"],
  [999999937, "4407190a254fdb5fa7f00aff69ba75b6c624bac6bc27096934e713506979384c"]
])("128x150x128 world for seed %d is byte-identical", (seed, expected) => {
  expect(hashBytes(makeWorld(128, 150, 128, seed).blocks)).toBe(expected);
});

test("full-size 512x150x512 world for seed 1337 is byte-identical...", () => {
  expect(hashBytes(fullWorld().blocks)).toBe("3675b077d5a0a677aadf7a4b5e781cfb38cec2eef6aa62c676e3a9f0f48a404f");
});
```

**How to add a `worldType` without breaking save compatibility:**

The **default generation path must remain byte-identical**. Here's the pattern:

```typescript
// Current signature (safe):
export function generateWorld(world: VoxelWorld): void { ... }

// Safe addition:
export function generateWorld(world: VoxelWorld, worldType?: WorldType): void {
  // Branch EARLY, BEFORE any block writes, based on worldType
  const config = worldType ? applyWorldTypeConfig(worldType) : GEN;
  
  generateTerrain(world, config);  // Pass config, not GEN
  // ... rest of generation
}

function applyWorldTypeConfig(type: WorldType): typeof GEN {
  if (type === "default") return GEN;  // Byte-identical path
  if (type === "amplified") return { ...GEN, biomes: { ...GEN.biomes, ... } };
  // ... etc
}
```

**The key invariant:**
- **If `worldType` is omitted/undefined or explicitly `"default"`, the generation must call no branches and hit the EXACT SAME code path as before**—byte-for-byte identical.
- The PRNG stream (`rand()`) must advance in the **same order** regardless, so even conditional branches that skip `rand()` calls break hashes.
- **Solution:** Use early exit/fork rather than conditional branches inside loops. OR consume the same number of `rand()` calls in both paths (gate unused values in a dummy variable).

**`WORLDGEN_VERSION` role** (lib/game/config.ts lines 166–174):
```typescript
export const WORLDGEN_VERSION = 7;
```

This constant **gates save compatibility**. When you change GEN, you must bump `WORLDGEN_VERSION`. The engine (lib/game/worlds.ts lines 74–84) compares `world.worldgenVersion` (saved with each world) against this constant:
- If `world.worldgenVersion < WORLDGEN_VERSION`, the world's block-diffs are **discarded** and it's **regenerated from the seed** (losing all player edits).
- If you add a `worldType` field, it should also go into `WorldMeta` and be compared similarly.

---

### 5. WHAT `generateWorld` RECEIVES & HOW TO THREAD A PARAMETER

**Current signature** (generation.ts line 67):
```typescript
export function generateWorld(world: VoxelWorld): void { ... }
```

**What `world` contains:**
- `world.seed: number` — used to seed the PRNG and noise functions
- `world.sizeX/Y/Z: number` — world dimensions (always 512/150/512)
- `world.blocks: Uint8Array` — voxel data (written during generation)
- `world.getBiome(x, z): BiomeId` — deterministic biome lookup (reads seed internally)

**Current construction** (GameEngine.ts lines 84–94):
```typescript
const seed = save?.seed ?? options.seed ?? Math.floor(Math.random() * 2147483647);
const size = options.worldSize ?? { x: WORLD_SIZE_X, y: WORLD_SIZE_Y, z: WORLD_SIZE_Z };
const world = new VoxelWorld(size.x, size.y, size.z, seed);
generateWorld(world);
```

**To add a `worldType` parameter:**

**Step 6: Update `generateWorld`** (generation.ts line 67):
```typescript
export function generateWorld(world: VoxelWorld): void {
  // EARLY FORK: set config before any block writes
  const config = world.worldType === "default" ? GEN : getWorldTypeConfig(world.worldType);
  
  const rand = (() => { /* unchanged */ })();
  
  generateTerrain(world, config);  // Pass config
  carveCaves(world, rand, config);
  // ... etc
}

function getWorldTypeConfig(type?: string): typeof GEN {
  if (!type || type === "default") return GEN;
  if (type === "amplified") return {
    ...GEN,
    biomes: Object.fromEntries(
      Object.entries(GEN.biomes).map(([k, v]) => [k, { ...v, noiseScale: v.noiseScale * 1.5 }])
    ) as typeof GEN.biomes
  };
  // ... more types
  return GEN;  // fallback
}
```

**Why this works:**
- If `worldType` is undefined/null/"default", the code hits **the same `GEN` path as before** → byte-identical hashes.
- Old saves without a `worldType` field load with `undefined`, which coerces to "default" → backward compatible.
- New saves can store the `worldType` and restore it on load.
- **No RNG stream breakage** because you fork *before* the first `rand()` call, and you use the same sequence afterward.

---

### VERDICT

**A. Current seed variety:**
- Pure seeds alone give **moderate variety** in detail (cave/ore/tree placement) but all worlds share the **same biome template and Y-ranges**.
- Every seed will have Mountains (~8–10% of map), Ocean (~5–15%), Desert (~5–10%), Forest (~15–20%), Plains (~50–60%).
- Sea level is always 43, mountains always peak >65, snow always >68, lava always y≤9.
- A "random seed" does **not** produce a dramatically different **world type** (e.g., you can't get a "desert world" or "island world" from seed variation alone).

**B. World type presets (cost vs. impact):**

| Preset | Config changes | Save impact | Implementation cost |
|--------|----------------|-------------|---------------------|
| **Amplified** | All `noiseScale ×1.5–2×` | Cheap—bump `WORLDGEN_VERSION`, early fork | Low |
| **Superflat** | All `noiseScale=0.5`, `baseHeight=50` | Cheap | Low |
| **Islands** | `seaLevel=90`, `baseHeight=45` | Cheap | Low |
| **Lush** | Biome threshold: `moisture > 0.1` | Cheap (only biome selection) | Low |
| **Deep** | `caveCount=500`, `caveChamberCount=300` | MEDIUM—RNG stream shifts | Medium (need versioning) |
| **Custom** | Slider for each GEN knob | Expensive—many new fields | High |

**Recommended approach:**
1. Add `worldType?: string` to `WorldMeta`, `VoxelWorld`, and `SaveData` (backward-compatible, optional field).
2. Fork generation in `generateWorld` *before* terrain, passing a config object rather than the global `GEN`.
3. For each preset, ensure the **default/"normal" path remains byte-identical** (no branching on the hot path for existing saves).
4. **Only bump `WORLDGEN_VERSION`** if you change the default path's constants or add/remove a generation step. Presets don't require a version bump if they're orthogonal branches.
5. Start with **Amplified** and **Superflat** (both safe, high-visibility impact, no RNG stream issues).

---

> AGENT

Good instinct — and exploring the worldgen turned up something that sharpens the question. Here's the honest picture:

**Every world is the same fixed 512×150×512 region with all 5 biomes baked in by fixed thresholds** (sea level always 43, mountains always present, etc.). So a different seed only reshuffles *detail* — it can't give you a "desert world" or an "island world." That means there are really two different features hiding behind "presets":

1. **Curated seeds** — a few hand-picked named seeds to click instead of typing. Cheap and zero-risk, **but** because seeds barely change a world's character here, the labels would be a bit of a fib and the payoff is small.
2. **World *types*** (like Minecraft's Default / Superflat / Amplified / Islands) — actual generation variants the player chooses. This is the version that genuinely delivers "let me pick a good world." It's more work and touches the worldgen determinism contract, but the exploration confirms it's tractable: fork `generateWorld` *before* any block write so the **Default path stays byte-identical** (existing worlds untouched), add a `worldType` field to the world record + save blob, and pin each new type with its own hash test. No forced reset of existing worlds.

My recommendation: if we do this, do **world types** — curated seeds underdeliver given how this engine generates. The cleanest first set is the ones that only touch terrain config (Superflat, Amplified, maybe Islands); biome-mix types (a lush or desert world) are more invasive because they touch biome selection, not just terrain.

4	/**
5	 * Deterministic terrain generation.
6	 *
7	 * ⚠ Saves store only the seed plus block-change deltas, so generateWorld() must
8	 * produce byte-identical output for a given seed forever. Generation order, the
9	 * PRNG, the noise functions, and every constant below are part of the save
10	 * format. generation.test.ts pins the output with SHA-256 digests — if it fails
11	 * after a change here, the change breaks existing saves.
12	 */
13

15	 * Every value here is part of the save format (see module header). Names exist
16	 * for readability; changing a value re-rolls every world.

52	function hash2D(x: number, z: number): number {
53	  const n = Math.sin(x * 127.1 + z * 311.7) * 43758.5453123;
54	  return n - Math.floor(n);
55	}
56	
57	function smoothNoise2D(x: number, z: number, seed: number): number {
58	  const s = seed * 0.013;
59	  const val =
60	    Math.sin(x * 0.015 + s) * 1.2 +
61	    Math.cos(z * 0.012 + s * 1.4) * 1.2 +
62	    Math.sin(x * 0.005 - z * 0.007 + s * 0.7) * 3.5 +
63	    Math.cos(x * 0.031 + z * 0.027 + s * 2.1) * 0.4;
64	  return val;
65	}
66	
67	export function generateWorld(world: VoxelWorld): void {
68	  const rand = (() => {
69	    let t = (world.seed >>> 0) + 0x6d2b79f5;
70	    return () => {
71	      t += 0x6d2b79f5;
72	      let r = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), 1 | t);
73	      r ^= r + Math.imul(r ^ (r >>> 7), 61 | r);
74	      return ((r ^ (r >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
75	    };
76	  })();
77	
78	  generateTerrain(world);
79	  carveCaves(world, rand);
80	  placeWater(world);
81	  placeBeaches(world);
82	  placeOres(world, rand);
83	  // After placeOres so it can't shift ore RNG (placeOres gates rand on air
84	  // adjacency, which lava would change), and before placeDungeons so dungeon
85	  // rooms — whose floors can sit at or below lavaLevel — overwrite any lava
86	  // rather than flooding. Trees/cacti/houses only read the surface, far above.
87	  placeLava(world);
88	  placeTrees(world, rand);
89	  placeCacti(world);
90	  placeStructures(world, rand);
91	  placeDungeons(world);
92	}

94	function generateTerrain(world: VoxelWorld): void {
95	  const maxX = world.sizeX - 1;
96	  const maxZ = world.sizeZ - 1;
97	
98	  // Fast clear and bedrock
99	  world.blocks.fill(BlockId.Air);
100	  for (let x = 0; x < world.sizeX; x += 1) {
101	    for (let z = 0; z < world.sizeZ; z += 1) {
102	      world.set(x, 0, z, BlockId.Bedrock);
103	    }
104	  }
105	
106	  for (let x = 0; x < world.sizeX; x += 1) {
107	    for (let z = 0; z < world.sizeZ; z += 1) {
108	      const biome = world.getBiome(x, z);
109	      const noise = smoothNoise2D(x, z, world.seed);
110	      const { baseHeight, noiseScale } = GEN.biomes[biome];
111	
112	      const topY = Math.max(5, Math.min(world.sizeY - 5, Math.floor(baseHeight + noise * noiseScale)));
113	      let topBlock = BlockId.Grass;
114	      if (biome === BiomeId.Desert || biome === BiomeId.Ocean) {
115	        topBlock = BlockId.Sand;
116	      } else if (biome === BiomeId.Mountains && topY > GEN.snowAboveY) {
117	        topBlock = BlockId.Snow;
118	      } else if (biome === BiomeId.Mountains && topY > GEN.mountainStoneAboveY) {
119	        topBlock = BlockId.Stone;
120	      }
121	
122	      for (let y = 1; y <= topY; y += 1) {
123	        if (y === topY) {
124	          world.set(x, y, z, topBlock);
125	        } else if (y > topY - 3) {
126	          world.set(x, y, z, topBlock === BlockId.Sand ? BlockId.Sand : BlockId.Dirt);
127	        } else if (y > topY - 8 && hash2D(x * 0.2 + y * 0.1, z * 0.2) > 0.88) {
128	          world.set(x, y, z, BlockId.Cobblestone);
129	        } else {
130	          world.set(x, y, z, BlockId.Stone);
131	        }
132	      }
133	    }
134	  }
135	
136	  // Unbreakable border walls.
137	  for (let y = 1; y <= GEN.borderWallHeight; y += 1) {
138	    for (let x = 0; x < world.sizeX; x += 1) {
139	      world.set(x, y, 0, BlockId.Bedrock);
140	      world.set(x, y, maxZ, BlockId.Bedrock);
141	    }
142	    for (let z = 0; z < world.sizeZ; z += 1) {
143	      world.set(0, y, z, BlockId.Bedrock);
144	      world.set(maxX, y, z, BlockId.Bedrock);
145	    }

149	function carveCaves(world: VoxelWorld, rand: () => number): void {
150	  const carveSphere = (cx: number, cy: number, cz: number, radius: number) => {
151	    const r2 = radius * radius;
152	    const minX = Math.max(1, Math.floor(cx - radius));
153	    const maxXc = Math.min(world.sizeX - 2, Math.ceil(cx + radius));
154	    const minY = Math.max(1, Math.floor(cy - radius));
155	    const maxYc = Math.min(world.sizeY - 2, Math.ceil(cy + radius));
156	    const minZ = Math.max(1, Math.floor(cz - radius));
157	    const maxZc = Math.min(world.sizeZ - 2, Math.ceil(cz + radius));
158	    for (let y = minY; y <= maxYc; y += 1) {
159	      for (let z = minZ; z <= maxZc; z += 1) {
160	        for (let x = minX; x <= maxXc; x += 1) {
161	          const dx = x - cx;
162	          const dy = y - cy;
163	          const dz = z - cz;
164	          if (dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz > r2) continue;
165	          const block = world.get(x, y, z);
166	          if (block !== BlockId.Bedrock) world.set(x, y, z, BlockId.Air);
167	        }
168	      }
169	    }
170	  };
171	
172	  for (let i = 0; i < GEN.caveCount; i += 1) {
173	    let x = 12 + rand() * (world.sizeX - 24);
174	    let y = 3 + rand() * (world.sizeY - 9);
175	    let z = 12 + rand() * (world.sizeZ - 24);
176	    let yaw = rand() * Math.PI * 2;
177	    let pitch = (rand() - 0.5) * 0.26;
178	    const length = 56 + Math.floor(rand() * 56);
179	    for (let step = 0; step < length; step += 1) {
180	      const r = 1.9 + rand() * 2.6;
181	      carveSphere(x, y, z, r);
182	      if (rand() > 0.968) carveSphere(x, y, z, 4.8 + rand() * 4.6);
183	      yaw += (rand() - 0.5) * 0.28;
184	      pitch = Math.max(-0.55, Math.min(0.55, pitch + (rand() - 0.5) * 0.16));
185	      x += Math.cos(yaw);
186	      z += Math.sin(yaw);
187	      y += Math.sin(pitch) * 0.8;
188	      if (x < 8 || x > world.sizeX - 8 || z < 8 || z > world.sizeZ - 8 || y < 2 || y > world.sizeY - 4) break;
189	    }
190	  }
191	
192	  for (let i = 0; i < GEN.caveChamberCount; i += 1) {
193	    const cx = 12 + rand() * (world.sizeX - 24);
194	    const cy = 5 + rand() * (world.sizeY - 14);
195	    const cz = 12 + rand() * (world.sizeZ - 24);
196	    carveSphere(cx, cy, cz, 5.4 + rand() * 6.4);
197	  }
198	}
199	
200	function placeLava(world: VoxelWorld): void {
201	  const { lavaLevel } = GEN;
202	  for (let x = 1; x < world.sizeX - 1; x += 1) {
203	    for (let z = 1; z < world.sizeZ - 1; z += 1) {
204	      for (let y = 1; y <= lavaLevel; y += 1) {
205	        if (world.get(x, y, z) === BlockId.Air) world.set(x, y, z, BlockId.Lava);
206	      }
207	    }
208	  }

211	function placeWater(world: VoxelWorld): void {
212	  const { seaLevel } = GEN;

214	    for (let z = 1; z < world.sizeZ - 1; z += 1) {

216	      if (topY >= seaLevel) continue;

218	        if (world.get(x, y, z) === BlockId.Air) world.set(x, y, z, BlockId.Water);

1	import * as THREE from "three";
2	import { BiomeId, BlockId, VoxelWorld } from "@/lib/world";
3	
4	export type SurfaceYAtFn = (x: number, z: number) => number;
5	
6	export function createSurfaceYAt(world: VoxelWorld): SurfaceYAtFn {
7	  return (x: number, z: number) => {
8	    const ix = Math.floor(x);
9	    const iz = Math.floor(z);
10	    if (ix < 0 || iz < 0 || ix >= world.sizeX || iz >= world.sizeZ) return 1;
11	    return world.highestSolidY(ix, iz) + 1;
12	  };
13	}
14	
15	/**
16	 * Deterministic spiral search for a safe spawn column: solid dry floor, two
17	 * air blocks for the body, gently sloped neighbors, preferring Plains. Falls
18	 * back to any biome, then to the search center.
19	 */
20	export function findSpawnOnLand(world: VoxelWorld, centerX: number, centerZ: number, seekPlains = true): { x: number; y: number; z: number } {
21	  const isGoodSpawn = (x: number, y: number, z: number): boolean => {
22	    const top = world.get(x, y - 1, z);
23	    const atBody = world.get(x, y, z);
24	    const atHead = world.get(x, y + 1, z);
25	    if (!world.isSolid(x, y - 1, z)) return false;
26	    if (atBody === BlockId.Water || atHead === BlockId.Water || top === BlockId.Water) return false;
27	    if (atBody !== BlockId.Air || atHead !== BlockId.Air) return false;
28	    if (seekPlains && world.getBiome(x, z) !== BiomeId.Plains) return false;
29	
30	    const h0 = world.highestSolidY(x + 1, z);
31	    const h1 = world.highestSolidY(x - 1, z);
32	    const h2 = world.highestSolidY(x, z + 1);
33	    const h3 = world.highestSolidY(x, z - 1);
34	    const ny = y - 1;
35	    return Math.abs(h0 - ny) <= 2 && Math.abs(h1 - ny) <= 2 && Math.abs(h2 - ny) <= 2 && Math.abs(h3 - ny) <= 2;
36	  };
37	
38	  const maxRadius = Math.min(300, Math.floor(Math.min(world.sizeX, world.sizeZ) * 0.4));
39	  for (let radius = 0; radius <= maxRadius; radius += 2) {
40	    for (let i = 0; i < 32; i += 1) {
41	      const angle = (Math.PI * 2 * i) / 32;
42	      const x = Math.max(5, Math.min(world.sizeX - 6, Math.floor(centerX + Math.cos(angle) * radius)));
43	      const z = Math.max(5, Math.min(world.sizeZ - 6, Math.floor(centerZ + Math.sin(angle) * radius)));
44	      const topY = world.highestSolidY(x, z);
45	      const y = topY + 1;
46	      if (isGoodSpawn(x, y, z)) return { x, y, z };
47	    }
48	  }
49	
50	  if (seekPlains) return findSpawnOnLand(world, centerX, centerZ, false);
51	
52	  const topY = world.highestSolidY(centerX, centerZ);
53	  return { x: centerX, y: topY + 1, z: centerZ };
54	}
55	
56	/** True when the surface position is dry: not a flooded column (seabed/lake floor). */
57	function isDrySurface(world: VoxelWorld, x: number, y: number, z: number): boolean {
58	  return world.get(Math.floor(x), y, Math.floor(z)) !== BlockId.Water;
59	}
60	
61	export function randomLandPoint(world: VoxelWorld, surfaceYAt: SurfaceYAtFn, rng: () => number = Math.random): THREE.Vector3 {
62	  for (let i = 0; i < 40; i += 1) {
63	    const x = 10 + rng() * (world.sizeX - 20);
64	    const z = 10 + rng() * (world.sizeZ - 20);
65	    const y = surfaceYAt(x, z);
66	    if (y > 2 && isDrySurface(world, x, y, z)) return new THREE.Vector3(x, y, z);
67	  }
68	  return new THREE.Vector3(world.sizeX / 2, 12, world.sizeZ / 2);
69	}
70	
71	export function randomLandPointNear(
72	  world: VoxelWorld,
73	  surfaceYAt: SurfaceYAtFn,
74	  centerX: number,
75	  centerZ: number,
76	  radius: number,
77	  rng: () => number = Math.random
78	): THREE.Vector3 {
79	  for (let i = 0; i < 50; i += 1) {
80	    const x = centerX + (rng() * 2 - 1) * radius;
81	    const z = centerZ + (rng() * 2 - 1) * radius;
82	    const clampedX = Math.max(10, Math.min(world.sizeX - 10, x));
83	    const clampedZ = Math.max(10, Math.min(world.sizeZ - 10, z));
84	    const y = surfaceYAt(clampedX, clampedZ);
85	    if (y > 2 && isDrySurface(world, clampedX, y, clampedZ)) return new THREE.Vector3(clampedX, y, clampedZ);
86	  }
87	  return randomLandPoint(world, surfaceYAt, rng);
88	}

271	function placeBeaches(world: VoxelWorld): void {

276	      if (world.get(x, topY, z) !== BlockId.Grass) continue;

280	        for (let dz = -2; dz <= 2 && !nearWater; dz += 1) {
281	          if (world.get(x + dx, GEN.seaLevel, z + dz) === BlockId.Water) nearWater = true;
282	        }
283	      }
284	      if (!nearWater) continue;

291	function placeTrees(world: VoxelWorld, rand: () => number): void {
292	  for (let i = 0; i < GEN.treeAttempts; i += 1) {
293	    const x = 4 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeX - 8));
294	    const z = 4 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeZ - 8));
295	
296	    const biome = world.getBiome(x, z);
297	    const spawnChance = GEN.biomes[biome].treeChance;
298	
299	    if (rand() > spawnChance) continue;
300	
301	    const topY = world.highestSolidY(x, z);
302	    if (world.get(x, topY, z) !== BlockId.Grass) continue;
303	
304	    const trunkHeight = spawnChance > 0.5 ? 4 + Math.floor(rand() * 3) : 3 + Math.floor(rand() * 3);
305	    for (let y = 1; y <= trunkHeight; y += 1) world.set(x, topY + y, z, BlockId.Wood);
306	
307	    const leafStart = topY + trunkHeight - 1;
308	    for (let ox = -2; ox <= 2; ox += 1) {
309	      for (let oz = -2; oz <= 2; oz += 1) {
310	        for (let oy = 0; oy <= 2; oy += 1) {
311	          const d = Math.abs(ox) + Math.abs(oz) + oy;
312	          if (d > 4) continue;
313	          // Only fill air: the canopy must not eat the trunk or neighbors.
314	          if (world.get(x + ox, leafStart + oy, z + oz) !== BlockId.Air) continue;
315	          world.set(x + ox, leafStart + oy, z + oz, BlockId.Leaves);
316	        }
317	      }
318	    }
319	  }
320	}
321	
322	function placeCacti(world: VoxelWorld): void {
323	  // Hash-gated scan instead of random attempts: density follows the dry
324	  // desert area, so a mostly-flooded desert gets few cacti instead of the
325	  // whole budget missing. Doesn't consume the shared rand stream.
326	  for (let x = 4; x < world.sizeX - 4; x += 1) {
327	    for (let z = 4; z < world.sizeZ - 4; z += 1) {
328	      if (hash2D(x * 1.7 + 11.3, z * 2.3 - 7.1) > GEN.cactusChance) continue;
329	      if (world.getBiome(x, z) !== BiomeId.Desert) continue;
```

1	import { BiomeId, BlockId, WORLD_SIZE_X, WORLD_SIZE_Y, WORLD_SIZE_Z } from "./blocks";
2	import { isDoorBlock } from "./doors";
3	
4	/**
5	 * Voxel data store plus cheap world queries. Terrain generation lives in
6	 * generation.ts, meshing in meshing.ts, raycast/collision in queries.ts.
7	 *
8	 * ⚠ Save-format invariants: saves address voxels through index(), and getBiome()
9	 * feeds terrain generation. Changing either breaks every existing save — see
10	 * generation.test.ts.
11	 */
12	export class VoxelWorld {
13	  readonly sizeX: number;
14	  readonly sizeY: number;
15	  readonly sizeZ: number;
16	  readonly seed: number;
17	  readonly blocks: Uint8Array;
18	  /**
19	   * Per-voxel light, packed (skyLight << 4) | blockLight, 0..15 each. A DERIVED
20	   * cache (never serialized): computeFullLight bakes it from blocks at load and
21	   * applyEdit patches it on block edits — see lighting.ts. Starts dark; the
22	   * engine bakes it once the block grid is final.
23	   */
24	  light: Uint8Array;
25	
26	  constructor(sizeX = WORLD_SIZE_X, sizeY = WORLD_SIZE_Y, sizeZ = WORLD_SIZE_Z, seed = 1337) {
27	    this.sizeX = sizeX;
28	    this.sizeY = sizeY;
29	    this.sizeZ = sizeZ;
30	    this.seed = seed;
31	    this.blocks = new Uint8Array(sizeX * sizeY * sizeZ);
32	    this.light = new Uint8Array(sizeX * sizeY * sizeZ);
33	  }
34	
35	  index(x: number, y: number, z: number): number {
36	    return x + z * this.sizeX + y * this.sizeX * this.sizeZ;
37	  }
38	
39	  inBounds(x: number, y: number, z: number): boolean {
40	    return x >= 0 && y >= 0 && z >= 0 && x < this.sizeX && y < this.sizeY && z < this.sizeZ;
41	  }
42	
43	  get(x: number, y: number, z: number): number {
44	    if (!this.inBounds(x, y, z)) return BlockId.Air;
45	    return this.blocks[this.index(x, y, z)];
```

The design is clean: fork `generateWorld` *before* any block write so the Default path stays byte-identical, and only three functions (`generateTerrain`, `placeWater`, `placeBeaches`) read a per-world terrain config. Let me write the plan.

  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/.claude/plans/i-am-thinking-of-splendid-sedgewick.md",
  "content": "# World Types in New-World Creation\n\n## Context\n\nThe new-world form (`components/menu/CreateWorldForm.tsx`, shipped in the worlds & profiles\nPR #21) only lets the player set a name and a seed (\"leave blank for random\"). But the\nworldgen produces **one fixed 512×150×512 region with all five biomes baked in by fixed\nthresholds** (sea level always 43, mountains always present) — so a different seed only\nreshuffles detail; it can't give a flat, amplified, or island world. The player can't\nactually choose the *character* of their world.\n\nThis adds **world types** to creation — **Default, Superflat, Amplified, Islands** (the\nterrain-config-only set; biome-biased types were explicitly excluded). Each is a real\ngeneration variant the player picks alongside name + seed. The type is fixed at creation\n(like the seed) and recorded on the world.\n\n**Determinism is preserved.** `generateWorld` forks on the type *before any block write*;\nthe Default branch uses the existing `GEN` values unchanged, so its output stays\nbyte-identical and **existing worlds and the current hash tests are untouched** — no\n`WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump. Each new type is independently deterministic and gets its own hash\nbaseline. (Terrain generation reads `smoothNoise2D`/`hash2D`, never the `rand` PRNG, so a\nterrain-config change can't desync the default stream — it only changes that type's own\ndownstream output, which is what we want.)\n\n## Design\n\n### 1. World-type definitions (world layer — `lib/world/`)\n\nNew `lib/world/worldTypes.ts` (no `GEN` dependency, so no import cycle):\n\n```ts\nexport type WorldType = \"default\" | \"flat\" | \"amplified\" | \"islands\";\nexport const WORLD_TYPE_IDS: readonly WorldType[] = [\"default\", \"flat\", \"amplified\", \"islands\"];\nexport function isWorldType(v: unknown): v is WorldType { /* membership check */ }\n```\n\nExport from the `lib/world/index.ts` barrel.\n\n### 2. Terrain config fork (`lib/world/generation.ts`)\n\n- `generateWorld(world)` → **`generateWorld(world, worldType: WorldType = \"default\")`** (optional\n  param ⇒ existing `generateWorld(world)` test calls stay \"default\" ⇒ byte-identical).\n- Add `terrainConfigFor(worldType)` returning `{ seaLevel, biomes: Record<BiomeId, {baseHeight, noiseScale}> }`.\n  `\"default\"` returns exactly `GEN.seaLevel` / `GEN.biomes` values (byte-identical path).\n- Thread the config into the **only three** functions that vary; everything else\n  (`carveCaves`, `placeOres`, `placeLava`, `placeTrees`, `placeCacti`, `placeStructures`,\n  `placeDungeons`) keeps reading `GEN` unchanged:\n  - `generateTerrain(world, cfg)` — read `cfg.biomes[biome]` (was `GEN.biomes[biome]`, line 110); keep `GEN.snowAboveY`/`mountainStoneAboveY`/`borderWallHeight`.\n  - `placeWater(world, cfg)` — `cfg.seaLevel` (was `GEN.seaLevel`, line 212).\n  - `placeBeaches(world, cfg)` — `cfg.seaLevel` (lines 275, 281).\n- Starting config values (tuned during impl so structural tests below pass):\n  - **flat**: `seaLevel 43`; every biome `{baseHeight: 48, noiseScale: 0}` → perfectly flat dry ground (above sea = no water), biome-colored top.\n  - **amplified**: `seaLevel 43`; `GEN` baseHeights, `noiseScale ≈ ×2` (mountains a touch less to limit clamping at `sizeY-5`).\n  - **islands**: `seaLevel ≈ 50`; land biomes nudged up so they form **gently-sloped** islands above a raised sea (e.g. Plains 52/3, Forest 54/4, Desert 51/3, Ocean 36/3, Mountains 62/14) — gentle so `findSpawnOnLand`'s ≤2 slope check still finds a dry spawn column.\n\n### 3. Persist the type (game layer)\n\n- `lib/game/worlds.ts`: add `worldType: WorldType` to `WorldMeta`; `sanitizeWorld` coerces\n  unknown → `\"default\"` (via `isWorldType`); `createWorld(profileId, name, seedInput, worldType: WorldType = \"default\", deps = {})`\n  stores it. Add UI metadata `WORLD_TYPE_PRESETS: { id: WorldType; label: string; blurb: string }[]`\n  (game/UI layer owns the labels; reuses the `WorldType` ids from `@/lib/world`).\n- `lib/game/types.ts`: add optional `worldType?: WorldType` to `SaveData` (additive, **version\n  stays 5**, no migration — absent ⇒ default).\n- `lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts`: add `worldType?` to `GameEngineOptions`; in the constructor\n  `const worldType = save?.worldType ?? options.worldType ?? \"default\"` then\n  `generateWorld(world, worldType)`; `serialize()` writes `worldType` (mirrors `seed`).\n  WorldMeta is authoritative; storing in the blob keeps it self-contained.\n- `lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts`: capture `worldTypeRef = opts.world.worldType` and pass\n  `worldType: worldTypeRef.current` alongside the existing `seed: worldSeedRef.current` in\n  `attachMount` (line ~150).\n\n### 4. UI (menu layer)\n\n- `components/menu/CreateWorldForm.tsx`: add a **type picker** above the seed field —\n  a swatch grid of `WORLD_TYPE_PRESETS` (label + blurb, `aria-pressed` for the selected),\n  defaulting to `\"default\"`, mirroring how `CreateProfileForm` renders the skin grid. Change\n  the submit callback to `onCreate(name, seed, worldType)`.\n- `components/menu/WorldSelect.tsx`: thread the type into `createWorld(profile.id, name, seed, worldType)`;\n  show the type on the world card sub-line for non-default (e.g. `Flat · Seed 1337`).\n- `app/menu.css`: a small `.world-type-grid` / `.world-type-option` built on the existing\n  `.mc-button` bevel + `aria-pressed` highlight (same pattern as `.skin-swatch`); zero assets.\n\n## Files\n\n**New:** `lib/world/worldTypes.ts` (+ barrel export), `lib/world/worldTypes.test.ts`.\n**Modified:** `lib/world/generation.ts` (fork + 3 threaded fns), `lib/world/generation.test.ts`\n(new-type hash baselines + structural tests), `lib/game/worlds.ts`, `lib/game/types.ts`,\n`lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts`, `lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts`, `components/menu/CreateWorldForm.tsx`,\n`components/menu/WorldSelect.tsx`, `app/menu.css`, the tests under `components/menu/` and\n`lib/game/worlds.test.ts`, plus docs.\n\n**Reuse:** `findSpawnOnLand` (`lib/game/spawn.ts:20` — already rejects water + steep slopes,\nwhich is why Islands must stay gently sloped); the skin-grid swatch pattern in\n`CreateProfileForm.tsx`; the existing `GEN` object for all non-terrain passes.\n\n## Determinism / save impact\n\n- **Default path stays byte-identical** — existing world hashes in `generation.test.ts` must\n  remain unchanged after the refactor (that's the regression guard). No `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump.\n- Each new type is pinned by its **own** SHA-256 baseline (generated once: temporarily log the\n  hash, run, paste — per the docs/testing.md re-baseline policy).\n- `worldType` becomes part of the save contract for non-default worlds (the engine must\n  regenerate with the same type the diffs were recorded against) — carried on `WorldMeta`\n  (authoritative) and in the `SaveData` blob (self-contained, like `seed`). Old worlds /\n  legacy-migrated \"My World\" have no `worldType` ⇒ `\"default\"` ⇒ correct.\n\n## Verification\n\n- `bun test`:\n  - `worldTypes.test.ts` — `isWorldType` membership/fallback.\n  - `worlds.test.ts` — `createWorld` stores the type; unknown coerces to default; update the\n    existing `createWorld(...)` call sites for the new positional arg.\n  - `generation.test.ts` — **default hashes unchanged**; new hash baselines for flat/amplified/islands;\n    structural probes: flat surface-height variance ≈ 0; amplified variance > default; islands has a\n    materially higher water-column fraction **and** `findSpawnOnLand` returns a column whose floor is\n    above sea level and not water (guards the Islands spawn risk).\n  - `CreateWorldForm.test.tsx` — picker defaults to Default, selecting a type and submitting passes it\n    to `onCreate`; `WorldSelect` forwards it to `createWorld`.\n- `bun run test:e2e` — extend `e2e/menu.e2e.ts`: create a **Flat** world, boot it, and sample a few\n  surface heights via `window.__monecraft` to assert the terrain near spawn is flat.\n- Full gate: `lint`, `typecheck`, `format:check`, `bun test`, `bun run build`, `bun run test:e2e`.\n- **Manual pass** (worldgen is visual — see docs/testing.md): eyeball each type once (flat plain,\n  amplified peaks, island archipelago with a dry spawn).\n- Docs with the change: `save-format.md` (worldType on WorldMeta + SaveData, default byte-identical,\n  new baselines), `testing.md` (new hash baselines + structural tests), `tuning.md` (the world-type\n  terrain configs), `manual.md` + `reference.md` (choosing a world type), `CHANGELOG.md`.\n\n## Commit slices (Conventional Commits; lint green each step)\n\n1. `feat(worldgen): add Default/Flat/Amplified/Islands world types` — `worldTypes.ts`, `terrainConfigFor`, `generateWorld(world, worldType)` + 3 threaded fns; default byte-identical; new hash + structural tests.\n2. `feat(save): persist world type on the world and save blob` — `WorldMeta`/`createWorld`/sanitize + `WORLD_TYPE_PRESETS`, `SaveData.worldType`, engine threading + serialize, `useMinecraftGame` passes it; unit tests.\n3. `feat(menu): choose a world type when creating a world` — `CreateWorldForm` picker, `WorldSelect` threading + card label, `menu.css`; component + e2e tests.\n4. `docs: document world types` — save-format, testing, tuning, manual, reference, CHANGELOG.\n"
}

Pulled the full review. CI is green, mergeable is clean, and there are **no required-reviewer blocks** — so PR #21 *can* merge now. CodeRabbit left 3 "Major" comments + 5 nitpicks. Here's my honest verdict on each, since a couple I'd actually push back on:

| # | CodeRabbit item | My take |
|---|---|---|
| **Major 1–2** | Gate `readProfiles`/`readWorlds` on `version === 1`, reject unknown → default | **Partially disagree.** Hard-rejecting an unknown version means a *future* v2 bump makes every user's profiles/worlds vanish (read as empty) until a migration ships — worse than today's behavior. Our readers already validate every field, so a forward-compatible read is safe against additive changes. I'd keep forward-compat and just **document it as deliberate** (+ a test pinning the behavior). |
| **Major 3** | `createWorld` should reject a non-existent `profileId` | **Skip.** `worlds.ts` deliberately doesn't import `profiles.ts` — the profile→world cascade is orchestrated in `GameShell` to keep the two manifests decoupled. The UI only ever calls `createWorld` with a real `profile.id`, so this guards a can't-happen case by reversing that layering. |
| **Nit: WorldSelect rename test** | Add coverage for the rename flow | **Apply.** Genuine gap — I tested ProfileSelect rename but not WorldSelect's. Cheap. |
| **Nit: e2e fixture hardcodes `worldgenVersion: 7`** | Pass the constant instead | **Apply (trivial).** Tidy; pass `WORLDGEN_VERSION` into `addInitScript`. (Harmless today — a drift just reboots the fresh fixture world from seed — but worth fixing.) |
| **Nit: smoke uses `worlds[0]` index lookup** | Use active-id lookup | **Skip/trivial.** The fixture has exactly one world; `worlds[0]` is stable. Low value. |
| **Nit: version-mismatch tests (×2)** | Add fallback tests | **Tie to Major 1–2** — add as behavior-pinning tests for whatever we decide there. |

Net: CI's green, nothing's actually blocking. The two "Major" items are design judgment calls where I lean against CodeRabbit, and the rest are minor.
