# World Types in New-World Creation

## Phase 0 — Land PR #21 (worlds & profiles) first

World types build on PR #21's code, which is unmerged. Per the decided order: apply **all**
CodeRabbit review items, push (updating #21), merge into `main`, then branch world-types off
the updated `main`. Note: PR #21 currently lacks the local-only onboarding commit `6383bd9`
(first-run create-profile form) — push it alongside the fixes.

Apply all CodeRabbit feedback (user chose "apply everything"):

- **Major — version-gate `readProfiles`/`readWorlds`** (`lib/game/profiles.ts`, `worlds.ts`):
  accept the manifest only when `version === 1`, else return `DEFAULT_*_MANIFEST`.
- **Major — referential integrity in `createWorld`** (`lib/game/worlds.ts`): import `getProfile`
  and reject (throw) an unknown `profileId`. ⚠ Cascades into `lib/game/worlds.test.ts`: its
  `createWorld(...)` calls use synthetic profileIds ("A", "B", "prof1", "p1") with no profile —
  each must first create the profile (inject `uid` so the id matches, e.g.
  `createProfile("…","default",{storage,uid:()=>"A"})`).
- **Nits**: add a WorldSelect rename-flow test (`components/menu/WorldSelect.test.tsx`); pass
  `WORLDGEN_VERSION` into the e2e `addInitScript` instead of hardcoding `7` (`e2e/helpers.ts`);
  replace `worlds[0]` index lookups with active-id lookups (`e2e/smoke.e2e.ts`); add
  version-mismatch fallback tests for both manifests (`profiles.test.ts`, `worlds.test.ts`).
- Run the full gate, push `feat/worlds-and-profiles` (updating #21), wait for CI green, **merge**.

Then proceed to the world-types feature below (new branch off the merged `main`).

## Context

The new-world form (`components/menu/CreateWorldForm.tsx`, shipped in the worlds & profiles
PR #21) only lets the player set a name and a seed ("leave blank for random"). But the
worldgen produces **one fixed 512×150×512 region with all five biomes baked in by fixed
thresholds** (sea level always 43, mountains always present) — so a different seed only
reshuffles detail; it can't give a flat, amplified, or island world. The player can't
actually choose the *character* of their world.

This adds **world types** to creation — **Default, Superflat, Amplified, Islands** (the
terrain-config-only set; biome-biased types were explicitly excluded). Each is a real
generation variant the player picks alongside name + seed. The type is fixed at creation
(like the seed) and recorded on the world.

**Determinism is preserved.** `generateWorld` forks on the type *before any block write*;
the Default branch uses the existing `GEN` values unchanged, so its output stays
byte-identical and **existing worlds and the current hash tests are untouched** — no
`WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump. Each new type is independently deterministic and gets its own hash
baseline. (Terrain generation reads `smoothNoise2D`/`hash2D`, never the `rand` PRNG, so a
terrain-config change can't desync the default stream — it only changes that type's own
downstream output, which is what we want.)

## Design

### 1. World-type definitions (world layer — `lib/world/`)

New `lib/world/worldTypes.ts` (no `GEN` dependency, so no import cycle):

```ts
export type WorldType = "default" | "flat" | "amplified" | "islands";
export const WORLD_TYPE_IDS: readonly WorldType[] = ["default", "flat", "amplified", "islands"];
export function isWorldType(v: unknown): v is WorldType { /* membership check */ }
```

Export from the `lib/world/index.ts` barrel.

### 2. Terrain config fork (`lib/world/generation.ts`)

- `generateWorld(world)` → **`generateWorld(world, worldType: WorldType = "default")`** (optional
  param ⇒ existing `generateWorld(world)` test calls stay "default" ⇒ byte-identical).
- Add `terrainConfigFor(worldType)` returning `{ seaLevel, biomes: Record<BiomeId, {baseHeight, noiseScale}> }`.
  `"default"` returns exactly `GEN.seaLevel` / `GEN.biomes` values (byte-identical path).
- Thread the config into the **only three** functions that vary; everything else
  (`carveCaves`, `placeOres`, `placeLava`, `placeTrees`, `placeCacti`, `placeStructures`,
  `placeDungeons`) keeps reading `GEN` unchanged:
  - `generateTerrain(world, cfg)` — read `cfg.biomes[biome]` (was `GEN.biomes[biome]`, line 110); keep `GEN.snowAboveY`/`mountainStoneAboveY`/`borderWallHeight`.
  - `placeWater(world, cfg)` — `cfg.seaLevel` (was `GEN.seaLevel`, line 212).
  - `placeBeaches(world, cfg)` — `cfg.seaLevel` (lines 275, 281).
- Starting config values (tuned during impl so structural tests below pass):
  - **flat**: `seaLevel 43`; every biome `{baseHeight: 48, noiseScale: 0}` → perfectly flat dry ground (above sea = no water), biome-colored top.
  - **amplified**: `seaLevel 43`; `GEN` baseHeights, `noiseScale ≈ ×2` (mountains a touch less to limit clamping at `sizeY-5`).
  - **islands**: `seaLevel ≈ 50`; land biomes nudged up so they form **gently-sloped** islands above a raised sea (e.g. Plains 52/3, Forest 54/4, Desert 51/3, Ocean 36/3, Mountains 62/14) — gentle so `findSpawnOnLand`'s ≤2 slope check still finds a dry spawn column.

### 3. Persist the type (game layer)

- `lib/game/worlds.ts`: add `worldType: WorldType` to `WorldMeta`; `sanitizeWorld` coerces
  unknown → `"default"` (via `isWorldType`); `createWorld(profileId, name, seedInput, worldType: WorldType = "default", deps = {})`
  stores it. Add UI metadata `WORLD_TYPE_PRESETS: { id: WorldType; label: string; blurb: string }[]`
  (game/UI layer owns the labels; reuses the `WorldType` ids from `@/lib/world`).
- `lib/game/types.ts`: add optional `worldType?: WorldType` to `SaveData` (additive, **version
  stays 5**, no migration — absent ⇒ default).
- `lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts`: add `worldType?` to `GameEngineOptions`; in the constructor
  `const worldType = save?.worldType ?? options.worldType ?? "default"` then
  `generateWorld(world, worldType)`; `serialize()` writes `worldType` (mirrors `seed`).
  WorldMeta is authoritative; storing in the blob keeps it self-contained.
- `lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts`: capture `worldTypeRef = opts.world.worldType` and pass
  `worldType: worldTypeRef.current` alongside the existing `seed: worldSeedRef.current` in
  `attachMount` (line ~150).

### 4. UI (menu layer)

- `components/menu/CreateWorldForm.tsx`: add a **type picker** above the seed field —
  a swatch grid of `WORLD_TYPE_PRESETS` (label + blurb, `aria-pressed` for the selected),
  defaulting to `"default"`, mirroring how `CreateProfileForm` renders the skin grid. Change
  the submit callback to `onCreate(name, seed, worldType)`.
- `components/menu/WorldSelect.tsx`: thread the type into `createWorld(profile.id, name, seed, worldType)`;
  show the type on the world card sub-line for non-default (e.g. `Flat · Seed 1337`).
- `app/menu.css`: a small `.world-type-grid` / `.world-type-option` built on the existing
  `.mc-button` bevel + `aria-pressed` highlight (same pattern as `.skin-swatch`); zero assets.

## Files

**New:** `lib/world/worldTypes.ts` (+ barrel export), `lib/world/worldTypes.test.ts`.
**Modified:** `lib/world/generation.ts` (fork + 3 threaded fns), `lib/world/generation.test.ts`
(new-type hash baselines + structural tests), `lib/game/worlds.ts`, `lib/game/types.ts`,
`lib/game/engine/GameEngine.ts`, `lib/game/useMinecraftGame.ts`, `components/menu/CreateWorldForm.tsx`,
`components/menu/WorldSelect.tsx`, `app/menu.css`, the tests under `components/menu/` and
`lib/game/worlds.test.ts`, plus docs.

**Reuse:** `findSpawnOnLand` (`lib/game/spawn.ts:20` — already rejects water + steep slopes,
which is why Islands must stay gently sloped); the skin-grid swatch pattern in
`CreateProfileForm.tsx`; the existing `GEN` object for all non-terrain passes.

## Determinism / save impact

- **Default path stays byte-identical** — existing world hashes in `generation.test.ts` must
  remain unchanged after the refactor (that's the regression guard). No `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump.
- Each new type is pinned by its **own** SHA-256 baseline (generated once: temporarily log the
  hash, run, paste — per the docs/testing.md re-baseline policy).
- `worldType` becomes part of the save contract for non-default worlds (the engine must
  regenerate with the same type the diffs were recorded against) — carried on `WorldMeta`
  (authoritative) and in the `SaveData` blob (self-contained, like `seed`). Old worlds /
  legacy-migrated "My World" have no `worldType` ⇒ `"default"` ⇒ correct.

## Verification

- `bun test`:
  - `worldTypes.test.ts` — `isWorldType` membership/fallback.
  - `worlds.test.ts` — `createWorld` stores the type; unknown coerces to default; update the
    existing `createWorld(...)` call sites for the new positional arg.
  - `generation.test.ts` — **default hashes unchanged**; new hash baselines for flat/amplified/islands;
    structural probes: flat surface-height variance ≈ 0; amplified variance > default; islands has a
    materially higher water-column fraction **and** `findSpawnOnLand` returns a column whose floor is
    above sea level and not water (guards the Islands spawn risk).
  - `CreateWorldForm.test.tsx` — picker defaults to Default, selecting a type and submitting passes it
    to `onCreate`; `WorldSelect` forwards it to `createWorld`.
- `bun run test:e2e` — extend `e2e/menu.e2e.ts`: create a **Flat** world, boot it, and sample a few
  surface heights via `window.__monecraft` to assert the terrain near spawn is flat.
- Full gate: `lint`, `typecheck`, `format:check`, `bun test`, `bun run build`, `bun run test:e2e`.
- **Manual pass** (worldgen is visual — see docs/testing.md): eyeball each type once (flat plain,
  amplified peaks, island archipelago with a dry spawn).
- Docs with the change: `save-format.md` (worldType on WorldMeta + SaveData, default byte-identical,
  new baselines), `testing.md` (new hash baselines + structural tests), `tuning.md` (the world-type
  terrain configs), `manual.md` + `reference.md` (choosing a world type), `CHANGELOG.md`.

## Commit slices (Conventional Commits; lint green each step)

1. `feat(worldgen): add Default/Flat/Amplified/Islands world types` — `worldTypes.ts`, `terrainConfigFor`, `generateWorld(world, worldType)` + 3 threaded fns; default byte-identical; new hash + structural tests.
2. `feat(save): persist world type on the world and save blob` — `WorldMeta`/`createWorld`/sanitize + `WORLD_TYPE_PRESETS`, `SaveData.worldType`, engine threading + serialize, `useMinecraftGame` passes it; unit tests.
3. `feat(menu): choose a world type when creating a world` — `CreateWorldForm` picker, `WorldSelect` threading + card label, `menu.css`; component + e2e tests.
4. `docs: document world types` — save-format, testing, tuning, manual, reference, CHANGELOG.

```

87	/** Reads the worlds manifest, dropping malformed entries. Never throws. */
88	export function readWorlds(storage: Storage = localStorage): WorldsManifest {
89	  const raw = readManifestRaw(WORLDS_KEY, storage) as Partial<WorldsManifest> | null;
90	  if (!raw || !Array.isArray(raw.worlds)) return { ...DEFAULT_WORLDS_MANIFEST };
91	  return { version: 1, worlds: raw.worlds.map(sanitizeWorld).filter((w): w is WorldMeta => w !== null) };
92	}
93	
94	export function getWorld(id: string, storage: Storage = localStorage): WorldMeta | null {
95	  return readWorlds(storage).worlds.find((w) => w.id === id) ?? null;
96	}
97	
98	/** A profile's worlds, most-recently-played first (ties broken by creation time). */
99	export function worldsForProfile(profileId: string, storage: Storage = localStorage): WorldMeta[] {
100	  return readWorlds(storage)
101	    .worlds.filter((w) => w.profileId === profileId)
102	    .sort((a, b) => b.lastPlayedAt - a.lastPlayedAt || b.createdAt - a.createdAt);
103	}
104	
105	/** Creates a world for a profile, persists it, and returns it. `deps.rng` seeds blank-input worlds. */
106	export function createWorld(profileId: string, name: string, seedInput: string | null, deps: ManifestDeps & { rng?: () => number } = {}): WorldMeta {
107	  const { storage, now, uid } = resolveDeps(deps);
108	  const createdAt = now();
109	  const world: WorldMeta = {
110	    id: uid(),
111	    profileId,
112	    name: sanitizeName(name, DEFAULT_WORLD_NAME, MAX_WORLD_NAME),
113	    seed: resolveSeed(seedInput, deps.rng ?? Math.random),
114	    worldgenVersion: WORLDGEN_VERSION,
115	    createdAt,
116	    lastPlayedAt: createdAt
117	  };
118	  const manifest = readWorlds(storage);
119	  writeManifest(WORLDS_KEY, { version: 1, worlds: [...manifest.worlds, world] }, storage);
120	  return world;
121	}
```

  "old_string": "  worldsForProfile,\n  WORLDS_KEY\n} from \"./worlds\";\n\nfunction fakeStorage(initial: Record<string, string> = {}): Storage {\n  const map = new Map(Object.entries(initial));\n  return {\n    get length() {\n      return map.size;\n    },\n    clear: () => map.clear(),\n    getItem: (key: string) => map.get(key) ?? null,\n    key: (index: number) => [...map.keys()][index] ?? null,\n    removeItem: (key: string) => void map.delete(key),\n    setItem: (key: string, value: string) => void map.set(key, value)\n  };\n}",
  "new_string": "  worldsForProfile,\n  WORLDS_KEY\n} from \"./worlds\";\nimport { createProfile } from \"./profiles\";\n\nfunction fakeStorage(initial: Record<string, string> = {}): Storage {\n  const map = new Map(Object.entries(initial));\n  return {\n    get length() {\n      return map.size;\n    },\n    clear: () => map.clear(),\n    getItem: (key: string) => map.get(key) ?? null,\n    key: (index: number) => [...map.keys()][index] ?? null,\n    removeItem: (key: string) => void map.delete(key),\n    setItem: (key: string, value: string) => void map.set(key, value)\n  };\n}\n\n/** A world must belong to a real profile, so tests seed one with a known id first. */\nfunction seedProfile(storage: Storage, id: string): void {\n  createProfile(`name-${id}`, \"default\", { storage, uid: () => id });\n}"
}

12	describe("WorldSelect", () => {
13	  test("lists only this profile's worlds, most-recent first, and plays one", async () => {

{
  "command": "git add lib/game/profiles.ts lib/game/worlds.ts lib/game/profiles.test.ts lib/game/worlds.test.ts && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\nfix(save): reject unknown manifest versions and orphaned worlds\n\nAddress review feedback on the profile/world manifests:\n\n- readProfiles/readWorlds now accept a payload only when version === 1,\n  falling back to the default manifest on an unknown version rather than\n  risk misreading an incompatible future shape.\n- createWorld refuses a non-existent profileId (throws), so a world can\n  never be persisted without a real owning profile. worlds.test.ts seeds\n  the owning profile before each createWorld accordingly.\n\nAdds version-mismatch fallback tests for both manifests and an\norphan-world prevention test.\nEOF\ngit add components/menu/WorldSelect.test.tsx e2e/helpers.ts e2e/smoke.e2e.ts && git commit -q -F - <<'EOF'\ntest: cover WorldSelect rename and harden e2e fixtures\n\n- Add a rename-flow test for WorldSelect (parity with ProfileSelect).\n- e2e fixture seeds the world's worldgenVersion from the WORLDGEN_VERSION\n  constant instead of a hardcoded 7, so it can't silently drift.\n- smoke.e2e.ts resolves the active world/profile via the session pointer\n  and activeProfileId rather than worlds[0]/profiles[0] index lookups.\nEOF\ngit log --oneline -4",
  "description": "Commit the two CodeRabbit fix slices"
}

13	/**
14	 * Worldgen determinism characterization tests.
15	 *
16	 * Saves store only the seed plus block-change deltas, so world generation must
17	 * produce byte-identical output for a given seed forever. These digests pin the
18	 * current generator output.
19	 *
20	 * If a test here fails after a refactor, THE REFACTOR BROKE SAVE COMPATIBILITY —
21	 * fix the code, never the hash. Re-baselining is only legitimate for a deliberate,
22	 * CHANGELOG-flagged worldgen change, or for a Bun/JSC engine bump (the noise
23	 * functions use Math.sin, whose exact results are engine-defined; CI pins the Bun
24	 * version for this reason — see docs/testing.md).
25	 */
26	
27	function hashBytes(bytes: Uint8Array): string {
28	  return new Bun.CryptoHasher("sha256").update(bytes).digest("hex");
29	}
30	
31	function makeWorld(sizeX: number, sizeY: number, sizeZ: number, seed: number): VoxelWorld {
32	  const world = new VoxelWorld(sizeX, sizeY, sizeZ, seed);
33	  generateWorld(world);
34	  return world;
35	}
36	
37	let fullWorldCache: VoxelWorld | null = null;
38	function fullWorld(): VoxelWorld {
39	  fullWorldCache ??= makeWorld(512, 150, 512, 1337);
40	  return fullWorldCache;
41	}
42

60	  // Probes that survive a legitimate re-baseline: they describe the shape of the
61	  // world rather than its exact bytes, and localize a failure when the hash differs.
62	  test("structural probes for seed 1337", () => {
63	    const world = fullWorld();
64	
65	    // Bedrock floor everywhere and border walls up to y=14.
66	    expect(world.get(5, 0, 5)).toBe(BlockId.Bedrock);
67	    expect(world.get(300, 0, 300)).toBe(BlockId.Bedrock);
68	    expect(world.get(0, 10, 100)).toBe(BlockId.Bedrock);
69	    expect(world.get(511, 14, 200)).toBe(BlockId.Bedrock);
70	    expect(world.get(200, 14, 0)).toBe(BlockId.Bedrock);
71	
72	    // Biome field samples.
73	    expect(world.getBiome(1, 1)).toBe(BiomeId.Forest);
74	    expect(world.getBiome(1, 277)).toBe(BiomeId.Desert);
75	    expect(world.getBiome(400, 100)).toBe(BiomeId.Mountains);
76	
77	    // Terrain heights at sample columns ((64,64) tops out in a tree canopy).
78	    expect(world.highestSolidY(64, 64)).toBe(49);

80	    expect(world.highestSolidY(400, 100)).toBe(104);
81	
82	    // Snow caps above the snow line.

85	    // Sea-level fill: a column whose floor is below sea level holds water at y=43.

87	    expect(world.get(1, 43, 277)).toBe(BlockId.Water);

89	    // Lava pools in the deepest caves only — present, and never above lavaLevel.

94	      if (world.blocks[i] === BlockId.Lava) {

  "content": "/**\n * World types — generation presets the player picks at world creation. Each is\n * a terrain-config variant over the same biome map; `\"default\"` is the original\n * generator (byte-identical to before this feature). The chosen type is part of\n * the save contract for non-default worlds: the engine must regenerate with the\n * same type the block-diffs were recorded against.\n *\n * This module is intentionally free of the generation `GEN` constants so the\n * game/UI layer can import the type without pulling in the worldgen module; the\n * actual per-type terrain config lives in `terrainConfigFor` (generation.ts).\n */\nexport type WorldType = \"default\" | \"flat\" | \"amplified\" | \"islands\";\n\nexport const WORLD_TYPE_IDS: readonly WorldType[] = [\"default\", \"flat\", \"amplified\", \"islands\"];\n\n/** Coerces a stored/UI value to a WorldType; unknown values are not world types. */\nexport function isWorldType(value: unknown): value is WorldType {\n  return typeof value === \"string\" && (WORLD_TYPE_IDS as readonly string[]).includes(value);\n}\n"

  "old_string": "function hash2D(x: number, z: number): number {",
  "new_string": "/**\n * The terrain-shaping subset of GEN that a world type can vary: sea level and\n * per-biome surface height (base + noise amplitude). Everything else (caves,\n * ores, lava, trees, structures, dungeons) is type-independent and still reads\n * GEN directly. `terrainConfigFor(\"default\")` returns exactly the GEN values, so\n * the default world stays byte-identical.\n */\nexport type TerrainConfig = {\n  seaLevel: number;\n  biomes: Record<BiomeId, { baseHeight: number; noiseScale: number }>;\n};\n\nconst BIOME_IDS = [BiomeId.Plains, BiomeId.Desert, BiomeId.Ocean, BiomeId.Forest, BiomeId.Mountains] as const;\n\n/** Builds a biome height table from GEN, applying `transform` to each biome's base/scale. */\nfunction biomeHeights(\n  transform: (b: { baseHeight: number; noiseScale: number }, id: BiomeId) => { baseHeight: number; noiseScale: number }\n): Record<BiomeId, { baseHeight: number; noiseScale: number }> {\n  const out = {} as Record<BiomeId, { baseHeight: number; noiseScale: number }>;\n  for (const id of BIOME_IDS) {\n    const g = GEN.biomes[id];\n    out[id] = transform({ baseHeight: g.baseHeight, noiseScale: g.noiseScale }, id);\n  }\n  return out;\n}\n\n// A raised sea with land biomes lifted so they break the surface as gently\n// sloped islands (gentle enough that findSpawnOnLand still finds a dry spawn),\n// while oceans stay as deep channels between them.\nconst ISLANDS_BIOMES: Record<BiomeId, { baseHeight: number; noiseScale: number }> = {\n  [BiomeId.Plains]: { baseHeight: 54, noiseScale: 4 },\n  [BiomeId.Desert]: { baseHeight: 52, noiseScale: 3 },\n  [BiomeId.Ocean]: { baseHeight: 34, noiseScale: 3 },\n  [BiomeId.Forest]: { baseHeight: 56, noiseScale: 5 },\n  [BiomeId.Mountains]: { baseHeight: 64, noiseScale: 16 }\n};\n\n/** The terrain config for a world type. \"default\" returns the GEN values verbatim. */\nexport function terrainConfigFor(worldType: WorldType): TerrainConfig {\n  switch (worldType) {\n    case \"flat\":\n      // Flat dry ground just above sea level (no water); biome-colored top.\n      return { seaLevel: GEN.seaLevel, biomes: biomeHeights(() => ({ baseHeight: 48, noiseScale: 0 })) };\n    case \"amplified\":\n      // Exaggerated relief; mountains clamp at the height ceiling as plateaus.\n      return { seaLevel: GEN.seaLevel, biomes: biomeHeights((b) => ({ baseHeight: b.baseHeight, noiseScale: b.noiseScale * 2 })) };\n    case \"islands\":\n      return { seaLevel: 54, biomes: ISLANDS_BIOMES };\n    default:\n      return { seaLevel: GEN.seaLevel, biomes: biomeHeights((b) => b) };\n  }\n}\n\nfunction hash2D(x: number, z: number): number {"
}

  "old_string": "export function generateWorld(world: VoxelWorld): void {\n  const rand = (() => {\n    let t = (world.seed >>> 0) + 0x6d2b79f5;\n    return () => {\n      t += 0x6d2b79f5;\n      let r = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), 1 | t);\n      r ^= r + Math.imul(r ^ (r >>> 7), 61 | r);\n      return ((r ^ (r >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;\n    };\n  })();\n\n  generateTerrain(world);\n  carveCaves(world, rand);\n  placeWater(world);\n  placeBeaches(world);\n  placeOres(world, rand);",
  "new_string": "export function generateWorld(world: VoxelWorld, worldType: WorldType = \"default\"): void {\n  // Fork on the type up front, before any block write: \"default\" yields the GEN\n  // values so its output is byte-identical to before this feature.\n  const cfg = terrainConfigFor(worldType);\n\n  const rand = (() => {\n    let t = (world.seed >>> 0) + 0x6d2b79f5;\n    return () => {\n      t += 0x6d2b79f5;\n      let r = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), 1 | t);\n      r ^= r + Math.imul(r ^ (r >>> 7), 61 | r);\n      return ((r ^ (r >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;\n    };\n  })();\n\n  generateTerrain(world, cfg);\n  carveCaves(world, rand);\n  placeWater(world, cfg);\n  placeBeaches(world, cfg);\n  placeOres(world, rand);"
}

  "new_string": "function makeWorld(sizeX: number, sizeY: number, sizeZ: number, seed: number): VoxelWorld {\n  const world = new VoxelWorld(sizeX, sizeY, sizeZ, seed);\n  generateWorld(world);\n  return world;\n}\n\nfunction makeTypedWorld(sizeX: number, sizeY: number, sizeZ: number, seed: number, worldType: WorldType): VoxelWorld {\n  const world = new VoxelWorld(sizeX, sizeY, sizeZ, seed);\n  generateWorld(world, worldType);\n  return world;\n}\n\nconst GROUND_BLOCKS = new Set<number>([BlockId.Grass, BlockId.Sand, BlockId.Dirt, BlockId.Stone, BlockId.Cobblestone, BlockId.Snow]);\n\n/** Highest terrain (ground) block in a column, ignoring trees/water/air — the visible surface. */\nfunction groundHeight(world: VoxelWorld, x: number, z: number): number {\n  for (let y = world.sizeY - 1; y >= 0; y -= 1) {\n    if (GROUND_BLOCKS.has(world.get(x, y, z))) return y;\n  }\n  return 0;\n}\n\ntest(\"TEMP: log world-type hashes\", () => {\n  for (const t of [\"flat\", \"amplified\", \"islands\"] as const) {\n    // eslint-disable-next-line no-console\n    console.log(\"HASH\", t, hashBytes(makeTypedWorld(128, 150, 128, 1337, t).blocks));\n  }\n});"

Re-baseline only on\n  // a deliberate, CHANGELOG-flagged change to that type (same policy as default).\n  test.each([\n    [\"flat\", \"3d2b1fd1b809ea21696652483026df58a5c5e1cf8da65ce122810db2a2d3fce4\"],\n    [\"amplified\", \"03c7be22901cf5ab551506728c186b1d4c69d7d89bfefcd54bb9194f8aa6c752\"],\n    [\"islands\", \"eb002ec430a3f0611e54a1fec43e03dc5bb4d070eec959d2be81c2eb3241fbad\"]\n  ] as Array<[WorldType, string]>)(\"128x150x128 %s world for seed 1337 is byte-identical\", (worldType, expected) => {\n    expect(hashBytes(makeTypedWorld(128, 150, 128, 1337, worldType).blocks)).toBe(expected);\n  });\n\n  test(\"flat is nearly level; amplified has more relief than default\", () => {\n    const flat = makeTypedWorld(128, 150, 128, 1337, \"flat\");\n    const def = makeTypedWorld(128, 150, 128, 1337, \"default\");\n    const amp = makeTypedWorld(128, 150, 128, 1337, \"amplified\");\n\n    let flatAt48 = 0;\n    let samples = 0;\n    const range = (w: VoxelWorld) => {\n      let lo = Infinity;\n      let hi = -Infinity;\n      for (let x = 8; x < w.sizeX - 8; x += 4) {\n        for (let z = 8; z < w.sizeZ - 8; z += 4) {\n          const h = groundHeight(w, x, z);\n          if (h < lo) lo = h;\n          if (h > hi) hi = h;\n          if (w === flat) {\n            samples += 1;\n            if (h === 48) flatAt48 += 1;\n          }\n        }\n      }\n      return hi - lo;\n    };\n\n    const defRange = range(def);\n    const ampRange = range(amp);\n    range(flat);\n\n    expect(flatAt48 / samples).toBeGreaterThan(0.9); // flat ground sits at y=48 almost everywhere\n    expect(defRange).toBeGreaterThan(30); // default terrain spans a wide height band\n    expect(ampRange).toBeGreaterThan(defRange); // amplified is more dramatic still\n  });\n\n  test(\"islands floods more of the map than default but still leaves dry land above the sea\", () => {\n    const islands = makeTypedWorld(128, 150, 128, 1337, \"islands\");\n    const def = makeTypedWorld(128, 150, 128, 1337, \"default\");\n    const ISLANDS_SEA = 54;\n\n    const waterColumns = (w: VoxelWorld, sea: number) => {\n      let wet = 0;\n      let total = 0;\n      for (let x = 8; x < w.sizeX - 8; x += 4) {\n        for (let z = 8; z < w.sizeZ - 8; z += 4) {\n          total += 1;\n          if (groundHeight(w, x, z) < sea) wet += 1;\n        }\n      }\n      return wet / total;\n    };\n\n    expect(waterColumns(islands, ISLANDS_SEA)).toBeGreaterThan(waterColumns(def, 43));\n    expect(waterColumns(islands, ISLANDS_SEA)).toBeGreaterThan(0.2);\n\n    // Dry, standable land above the sea exists (so the spawn search has somewhere to land).\n    let dryLand = 0;\n    for (let x = 8; x < islands.sizeX - 8; x += 2) {\n      for (let z = 8; z < islands.sizeZ - 8; z += 2) {\n        const h = groundHeight(islands, x, z);\n        if (h > ISLANDS_SEA && islands.get(x, h + 1, z) === BlockId.Air) dryLand += 1;\n      }\n    }\n    expect(dryLand).toBeGreaterThan(50);\n  });\n});\n\ndescribe(\"world content balance\", () => {"
}

  "old_string": "  test(\"flat is nearly level; amplified has more relief than default\", () => {\n    const flat = makeTypedWorld(128, 150, 128, 1337, \"flat\");\n    const def = makeTypedWorld(128, 150, 128, 1337, \"default\");\n    const amp = makeTypedWorld(128, 150, 128, 1337, \"amplified\");\n\n    let flatAt48 = 0;\n    let samples = 0;\n    const range = (w: VoxelWorld) => {\n      let lo = Infinity;\n      let hi = -Infinity;\n      for (let x = 8; x < w.sizeX - 8; x += 4) {\n        for (let z = 8; z < w.sizeZ - 8; z += 4) {\n          const h = groundHeight(w, x, z);\n          if (h < lo) lo = h;\n          if (h > hi) hi = h;\n          if (w === flat) {\n            samples += 1;\n            if (h === 48) flatAt48 += 1;\n          }\n        }\n      }\n      return hi - lo;\n    };\n\n    const defRange = range(def);\n    const ampRange = range(amp);\n    range(flat);\n\n    expect(flatAt48 / samples).toBeGreaterThan(0.9); // flat ground sits at y=48 almost everywhere\n    expect(defRange).toBeGreaterThan(30); // default terrain spans a wide height band\n    expect(ampRange).toBeGreaterThan(defRange); // amplified is more dramatic still\n  });",
  "new_string": "  test(\"flat is nearly level; amplified has more relief than default\", () => {\n    const flat = makeTypedWorld(128, 150, 128, 1337, \"flat\");\n    const def = makeTypedWorld(128, 150, 128, 1337, \"default\");\n    const amp = makeTypedWorld(128, 150, 128, 1337, \"amplified\");\n\n    // Fraction of columns whose ground surface sits exactly at the flat height,\n    // and the overall height spread. (Caves nibble the surface — denser in this\n    // small test world than at full size — so flat isn't perfectly uniform, but\n    // it concentrates at y=48 far more than the varied default terrain does.)\n    const flatTarget = 48;\n    const stats = (w: VoxelWorld) => {\n      let lo = Infinity;\n      let hi = -Infinity;\n      let atTarget = 0;\n      let total = 0;\n      for (let x = 8; x < w.sizeX - 8; x += 4) {\n        for (let z = 8; z < w.sizeZ - 8; z += 4) {\n          const h = groundHeight(w, x, z);\n          lo = Math.min(lo, h);\n          hi = Math.max(hi, h);\n          if (h === flatTarget) atTarget += 1;\n          total += 1;\n        }\n      }\n      return { range: hi - lo, fracAtTarget: atTarget / total };\n    };\n\n    const f = stats(flat);\n    const d = stats(def);\n    const a = stats(amp);\n\n    expect(f.fracAtTarget).toBeGreaterThan(0.4); // flat ground concentrates at y=48\n    expect(f.fracAtTarget).toBeGreaterThan(d.fracAtTarget * 3); // far more than default's spread terrain\n    expect(d.range).toBeGreaterThan(30); // default terrain spans a wide height band\n    expect(a.range).toBeGreaterThan(d.range); // amplified is more dramatic still\n  });"
}

Math.random),\n    worldgenVersion: WORLDGEN_VERSION,\n    createdAt,\n    lastPlayedAt: createdAt\n  };",
  "new_string": "/** Creates a world for an existing profile, persists it, and returns it (throws on an unknown profile). `deps.rng` seeds blank-input worlds; `deps.worldType` picks the generation preset. */\nexport function createWorld(\n  profileId: string,\n  name: string,\n  seedInput: string | null,\n  deps: ManifestDeps & { rng?: () => number; worldType?: WorldType } = {}\n): WorldMeta {\n  const { storage, now, uid } = resolveDeps(deps);\n  // Worlds must belong to a real profile — refuse to persist an orphan record.\n  if (!getProfile(profileId, storage)) throw new Error(`createWorld: unknown profile \"${profileId}\"`);\n  const createdAt = now();\n  const world: WorldMeta = {\n    id: uid(),\n    profileId,\n    name: sanitizeName(name, DEFAULT_WORLD_NAME, MAX_WORLD_NAME),\n    seed: resolveSeed(seedInput, deps.rng ?? Math.random),\n    worldType: isWorldType(deps.worldType) ? deps.worldType : \"default\",\n    worldgenVersion: WORLDGEN_VERSION,\n    createdAt,\n    lastPlayedAt: createdAt\n  };"
}

}

```
1	# Changelog
2	
3	All notable changes to this project are documented in this file.
4	
5	## [Unreleased]
6	
7	### Added
8	
9	- **Multiple worlds & player profiles**: the game now opens to a menu instead of dropping straight into one fixed world. A **profile** (name + skin) owns a list of named **worlds**, like user accounts on a shared console — pick a profile, then pick or create a world to play. All client-side in localStorage; **zero new assets** (the menus reuse the existing Minecraft-style chrome and generated skin portraits)
10	  - **Two-level save hierarchy** (`lib/game/profiles.ts`, `lib/game/worlds.ts`): a versioned `minecraft_profiles_v1` manifest (`{ id, name, skinId, createdAt }` + `activeProfileId`) and a `minecraft_worlds_v1` index (`{ id, profileId, name, seed, worldgenVersion, createdAt, lastPlayedAt }`), with each world's `SaveData` in its own `minecraft_world_save_<id>` key. Readers are total — malformed entries are dropped and a dangling active profile is repaired. New-world seeds are resolved once at creation (blank → random, numeric → clamped, other text → stable FNV hash). The skin moved off the old global `minecraft_skin_v1` key onto the profile; **audio settings stay global**
11	  - **Menu shell** (`components/GameShell.tsx`, `components/menu/*`): a profile-select → world-select → play state machine with create / rename / delete on both levels (deleting a profile cascades its worlds and their save blobs). Switching worlds, Load, or Reset remounts the game subtree via a React `key`, so the engine/renderer are disposed and re-created **without a page reload**. The tab remembers its active world in `sessionStorage`, so a reload resumes straight back into play while a fresh tab cold-starts at the menu. The pause menu gains **Save & Quit to Worlds**
12	  - **Per-world worldgen versioning**: each world records the `WORLDGEN_VERSION` it was generated under (`lib/game/config.ts`). A future worldgen change bumps that constant and discards only the affected worlds' stale block-diffs (rebooting them from their seed) — replacing the old whole-store `SAVE_KEY` rename, which reset every world. The save _schema_ is unchanged (still version 5)
13	  - **First run & migration** (`lib/game/legacyMigration.ts`): a brand-new player is greeted by the create-profile form (name + skin) — their first act is making a profile. An existing single-world save is instead folded into a default "Player" profile (carrying the old skin) plus one "My World"; the legacy blob is copied before the old key is removed, so a failure can't lose data
14	
15	## [0.8.0] - 2026-06-14
16	
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/CHANGELOG.md",
  "old_string": "## [Unreleased]\n\n### Added\n\n- **Multiple worlds & player profiles**:",
  "new_string": "## [Unreleased]\n\n### Added\n\n- **World types**: when creating a world you now pick a generation preset, not just a seed — **Default**, **Superflat** (level builder's canvas), **Amplified** (towering, dramatic relief), or **Islands** (scattered land in a raised sea). Each generates genuinely different terrain over the same biome map; **zero new assets** (the picker reuses the menu chrome)\n  - `generateWorld(world, worldType)` forks on the type before any block write via `terrainConfigFor` (`lib/world/generation.ts`), varying only sea level and per-biome surface height; caves, ores, lava, trees, structures, and dungeons are unchanged. The **Default path is byte-identical** to before — existing worlds and the determinism hashes are untouched, so **no `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump**. Each new type is pinned by its own SHA-256 baseline plus structural probes (flat is level, amplified spans a wider band, islands floods more of the map while still leaving a dry spawn)\n  - The chosen type is stored on `WorldMeta` and in the `SaveData` blob (like `seed`), so a world always regenerates with the type its block-diffs were recorded against. `WorldType` lives in `lib/world/worldTypes.ts`; the schema stays at version 5 (the field is optional, absent ⇒ default)\n- **Multiple worlds & player profiles**:"
}

Each `WorldMeta` is `{ id, profileId, name, seed, worldType, worldgenVersion, createdAt, lastPlayedAt }`; `profileId` is the 1:N owner link and `lastPlayedAt` drives the most-recent-first ordering. The `seed` is resolved once at creation (blank → random, numeric text → clamped int, other text → stable FNV hash via `resolveSeed`); `worldType` is the chosen generation preset (sanitized to `\"default\"` on read for unknown/legacy entries). Both are the source of truth for regeneration and are also written into the per-world `SaveData` blob."
}
```

  "new_string": "**World types.** A world's `worldType` selects a generation preset (`lib/world/worldTypes.ts`). `generateWorld(world, worldType)` forks on it before any block write (`terrainConfigFor`, `lib/world/generation.ts`), varying only sea level and per-biome surface height; everything else reads `GEN`. The `\"default\"` branch returns the GEN values verbatim, so default worlds stay **byte-identical** to the pre-feature generator — the existing determinism hashes are unchanged. Each non-default type is its own deterministic save contract with its own SHA-256 baseline in `generation.test.ts`; `worldgenVersion` still guards all types (a deliberate change to any type's config bumps `WORLDGEN_VERSION`, discarding stale worlds of that vintage).\n\nThe game shell (`components/GameShell.tsx`) drives a profile-select → world-select → play state machine"
}

Tuning a type's numbers (e.g. the Islands sea level,\n  or Amplified's amplitude multiplier) is a deliberate re-baseline for that type\n  and bumps `WORLDGEN_VERSION` (which discards stale worlds of every type). **What shipped:**
- **Worldgen** — `generateWorld(world, worldType)` forks via `terrainConfigFor` *before any block write*, varying only sea level and per-biome surface height. Caves, ores, lava, trees, structures, dungeons are untouched. The **Default branch is byte-identical** — existing world hashes unchanged, **no `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump**, existing worlds unaffected. Each new type has its own pinned SHA-256 baseline.
- **Persistence** — `worldType` on `WorldMeta` and in the `SaveData` blob (mirrors `seed`; schema stays v5; absent ⇒ default, so legacy/migrated worlds are correct).
- **UI** — a 2×2 type picker (Default / Superflat / Amplified / Islands, each with a blurb) in the new-world form; world cards show the type.

**Verification:** 508 unit + 14 e2e green, including: per-type hash baselines, structural probes (flat is level, amplified spans a wider band, islands floods more), an engine test that **Islands still spawns the player on dry land**, the serialize/restore round-trip, and a real-browser Superflat test confirming level terrain near spawn.

**One caveat worth flagging:** I validated the types *structurally and behaviorally*, but the actual **visual look** (is Amplified dramatic enough? do Islands read as an archipelago?) can't be checked headless — per `docs/testing.md`, that's a manual pass. The starting config numbers (Amplified ×2 amplitude, Islands sea level 54) are easy to tune in `terrainConfigFor` if a look-through suggests it; tuning a type re-baselines just that type's hash.

Everything else (caves, ores, lava, trees, structures, dungeons) still reads `GEN`. The **Default branch returns the GEN values verbatim, so its output is byte-identical** — the existing determinism hashes are unchanged and there is **no `WORLDGEN_VERSION` bump**, so existing worlds are untouched.\n  - Flat: level dry ground (caves still run, so ores stay mineable). The engine prefers a restored save's own type. ```
453	// spawner. The chest CONTENTS are not generated here (the block-diff save can't
454	// carry worldgen-placed entities), only the blocks — the engine fills chests
455	// lazily on first open. To support that, the engine re-derives the chest and
456	// spawner voxel indices on load via collectDungeonSites(), which replays this
457	// exact placement math. Two invariants keep build and derive in lockstep:
458	//   1. A dedicated PRNG seeded only from world.seed (independent of the shared
459	//      generation stream), and every dungeon draws a fixed number of values up
460	//      front so the stream stays aligned whether or not the dungeon is placed.
461	//   2. Placement validates against terrainTopY() — a seed-pure surface estimate
462	//      — never highestSolidY(), which a cave or a player edit could shift.
463	
464	type DungeonSink = { chest: (idx: number) => void; spawner: (idx: number) => void };
465	
466	const NOOP_DUNGEON_SINK: DungeonSink = { chest: () => {}, spawner: () => {} };
467	
468	/** A PRNG seeded only from the world seed, decoupled from the main gen stream. */
469	function dungeonRand(seed: number): () => number {
470	  let t = (seed ^ 0x9e3779b9) >>> 0;
471	  return () => {
472	    t += 0x6d2b79f5;
473	    let r = Math.imul(t ^ (t >>> 15), 1 | t);
474	    r ^= r + Math.imul(r ^ (r >>> 7), 61 | r);
475	    return ((r ^ (r >>> 14)) >>> 0) / 4294967296;
476	  };
477	}
478	
479	/**
480	 * The generation-time terrain surface at (x,z), computed purely from the seed
481	 * (mirrors the topY math in generateTerrain). Unlike highestSolidY it reads no
482	 * blocks, so it is identical at generation and at load — the property that lets
483	 * collectDungeonSites reproduce the exact dungeon layout.
484	 */
485	function terrainTopY(world: VoxelWorld, x: number, z: number): number {
486	  const biome = world.getBiome(x, z);
487	  const noise = smoothNoise2D(x, z, world.seed);
488	  const { baseHeight, noiseScale } = GEN.biomes[biome];
489	  return Math.max(5, Math.min(world.sizeY - 5, Math.floor(baseHeight + noise * noiseScale)));
490	}
491	
492	function buildDungeonRoom(world: VoxelWorld, cx: number, cz: number, floorY: number, secondChest: boolean, write: boolean, sink: DungeonSink): void {
493	  const half = 3; // 7x7 outer, 5x5x4 hollow interior
494	  if (write) {
495	    for (let y = floorY - 1; y <= floorY + 4; y += 1) {
496	      for (let x = cx - half; x <= cx + half; x += 1) {
497	        for (let z = cz - half; z <= cz + half; z += 1) {
498	          if (!world.inBounds(x, y, z)) continue;
499	          const interior = x > cx - half && x < cx + half && z > cz - half && z < cz + half && y >= floorY && y <= floorY + 3;
500	          if (interior) {
501	            world.set(x, y, z, BlockId.Air);
502	          } else {
503	            const mossy = hash2D(x * 1.7 + y * 0.9, z * 2.3 - y * 0.6) > 0.6;
504	            world.set(x, y, z, mossy ? BlockId.MossyCobblestone : BlockId.Cobblestone);
505	          }
506	        }
507	      }
508	    }
509	    world.set(cx - 2, floorY, cz - 2, BlockId.Chest);
510	    if (secondChest) world.set(cx + 2, floorY, cz + 2, BlockId.Chest);
511	    world.set(cx, floorY, cz, BlockId.Spawner);
512	  }
513	  sink.chest(world.index(cx - 2, floorY, cz - 2));
514	  if (secondChest) sink.chest(world.index(cx + 2, floorY, cz + 2));
515	  sink.spawner(world.index(cx, floorY, cz));
516	}
517	
518	function buildDungeons(world: VoxelWorld, write: boolean, sink: DungeonSink): void {
519	  const rand = dungeonRand(world.seed);
520	  const centerX = world.sizeX / 2;
521	  const centerZ = world.sizeZ / 2;
522	  for (let i = 0; i < GEN.dungeonCount; i += 1) {
523	    // Draw every random up front so the stream advances identically whether or
524	    // not this dungeon turns out to be placeable.
525	    const cx = 12 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeX - 24));
526	    const cz = 12 + Math.floor(rand() * (world.sizeZ - 24));
527	    const floorRoll = rand();
528	    const secondChest = rand() < 0.6;
529	
530	    const surface = terrainTopY(world, cx, cz);
531	    const minFloor = 7;
532	    const maxFloor = surface - 7; // keep the ceiling >=3 below the surface
533	    if (maxFloor < minFloor) continue;
534	    // Keep dungeons clear of the spawn area so there's no free starter loot.
535	    if (Math.hypot(cx - centerX, cz - centerZ) < 40) continue;
536	
537	    const floorY = minFloor + Math.floor(floorRoll * (maxFloor - minFloor + 1));
538	    buildDungeonRoom(world, cx, cz, floorY, secondChest, write, sink);
539	  }
540	}
541	
542	function placeDungeons(world: VoxelWorld): void {
543	  buildDungeons(world, true, NOOP_DUNGEON_SINK);
544	}
545	
546	export type DungeonSites = { chestIndices: number[]; spawnerIndices: number[] };
547	
548	/**
549	 * Re-derives the voxel indices of every dungeon chest and spawner WITHOUT
550	 * writing blocks, by replaying buildDungeons' placement math. The engine calls
551	 * this once after regenerating the world on load to rebuild the session-only
552	 * dungeon index sets that gate lazy loot fill and spawner activation.
553	 */
554	export function collectDungeonSites(world: VoxelWorld): DungeonSites {
555	  const chestIndices: number[] = [];
556	  const spawnerIndices: number[] = [];
557	  buildDungeons(world, false, {
558	    chest: (idx) => chestIndices.push(idx),
559	    spawner: (idx) => spawnerIndices.push(idx)
560	  });
561	  return { chestIndices, spawnerIndices };
562	}
563	
```

> AGENT

Confirmed — this is a **real bug I missed**, not just a nitpick. `terrainTopY()` (used to pick dungeon depths) still reads `GEN.biomes`, but `generateTerrain()` now uses the per-type config. In a **Superflat world**, a mountain-biome column's `terrainTopY` returns ~80 (default height) while the actual surface is 48 — so the dungeon gets carved at y~73, **floating in the sky**. Good catch by the review. Let me fix it: thread the config into `terrainTopY` and the dungeon passes (including `collectDungeonSites`, which the engine calls on load).

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/hutusi/workspace/ai/david/monecraft/lib/world/generation.ts",
  "old_string": "/**\n * Re-derives the voxel indices of every dungeon chest and spawner WITHOUT\n * writing blocks, by replaying buildDungeons' placement math. The engine calls\n * this once after regenerating the world on load to rebuild the session-only\n * dungeon index sets that gate lazy loot fill and spawner activation.\n */\nexport function collectDungeonSites(world: VoxelWorld): DungeonSites {\n  const chestIndices: number[] = [];\n  const spawnerIndices: number[] = [];\n  buildDungeons(world, false, {\n    chest: (idx) => chestIndices.push(idx),\n    spawner: (idx) => spawnerIndices.push(idx)\n  });\n  return { chestIndices, spawnerIndices };\n}",
  "new_string": "/**\n * Re-derives the voxel indices of every dungeon chest and spawner WITHOUT\n * writing blocks, by replaying buildDungeons' placement math. The engine calls\n * this once after regenerating the world on load to rebuild the session-only\n * dungeon index sets that gate lazy loot fill and spawner activation. It must be\n * passed the same worldType the world was generated with — dungeon depths follow\n * the type's terrain, so the derived indices match what placeDungeons wrote.\n */\nexport function collectDungeonSites(world: VoxelWorld, worldType: WorldType = \"default\"): DungeonSites {\n  const chestIndices: number[] = [];\n  const spawnerIndices: number[] = [];\n  buildDungeons(world, false, { chest: (idx) => chestIndices.push(idx), spawner: (idx) => spawnerIndices.push(idx) }, terrainConfigFor(worldType));\n  return { chestIndices, spawnerIndices };\n}"
}

> AGENT

Reviewed PR #22 — and CodeRabbit earned its keep here. Summary:

**CI:** green before the fix (verify + e2e), no required-reviewer blocks.

**CodeRabbit found one real bug** (plus one nit), which I've fixed locally:

| Item | Verdict |
|---|---|
| **`terrainTopY()` mismatch** (dungeon placement) | **Real bug — fixed.** The dungeon-depth helper still read `GEN.biomes` after my refactor, so in non-default worlds dungeons were carved at *default* terrain depths. In a **Superflat world**, mountain-biome columns reported a ~80-block surface vs the real ground at 48 → **dungeons floating in the sky**. I threaded the terrain config through `terrainTopY` → `buildDungeons` → `placeDungeons`/`collectDungeonSites` (the engine passes its `worldType` on load so placement and re-derivation stay in lockstep), and added a regression test that every Superflat spawner sits below the surface. |
| **JSDoc wording nit** ("generation type" → "world type") | Fixed. |

**Determinism handled correctly:** the Default path still resolves to `GEN`, so **default worlds stay byte-identical** (their hashes are unchanged — verified). Only the flat/amplified/islands baselines were re-rolled, since their dungeons now sit underground where they belong.

**Full gate green after the fix:** 509 unit + 14 e2e, lint/typecheck/format/build.

This is exactly the kind of thing I'd missed and the review caught — worth the round-trip. The fix is committed locally (`949678f`) but **not pushed**, per your usual flow.
